The framework first employs a fast-thinking model to generate confidence-labeled responses, enabling immediate use of high-confidence outputs. For uncertain predictions, a slow refinement model conducts targeted reasoning to improve accuracy. To align model behavior with our proposed object, we propose a hybrid training pipeline, enhancing self-awareness without degrading task performance. Evaluations on dialogue state tracking tasks demonstrate that EKBM achieves superior model reliability over uncertainty-based baselines. Further analysis reveals that refinement substantially boosts accuracy while maintaining low computational overhead. Our work establishes a scalable paradigm for advancing LLM reliability and balancing accuracy and practical utility in error-sensitive applications.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the paper "Enhancing LLM Reliability via Explicit Knowledge Boundary Modeling":

**Summary:**

This paper proposes a framework called Explicit Knowledge Boundary Modeling (EKBM) to improve the reliability of Large Language Models (LLMs) by addressing hallucination issues. The framework integrates a fast-thinking model to generate confidence-labeled responses and a slow-thinking refinement model for uncertain predictions. This approach seeks to balance immediate usability with comprehensive accuracy. The paper introduces the concepts of "sure" and "unsure" outputs, aiming to deliver near-perfect accuracy for high-confidence predictions while providing helpful information even for uncertain predictions. The method employs a hybrid training pipeline to enhance self-awareness without degrading task performance, with evaluations conducted on dialogue state tracking tasks. The paper demonstrates that EKBM achieves superior model reliability compared to uncertainty-based baselines while keeping computational overhead low.

**Critical Evaluation:**

*   **Novelty:** The paper's novelty lies primarily in the specific combination of techniques. Explicitly modeling knowledge boundaries isn't entirely new, as various methods attempt to estimate model confidence or reject out-of-scope queries. However, the EKBM framework integrates fast/slow reasoning with confidence labeling and a dedicated refinement model, seeking a more balanced approach. The introduction of "sure" and "unsure" categories and the corresponding training and evaluation metrics represent a valuable refinement of the approach to reliability.

*   **Significance:** The paper addresses a critical and well-recognized issue in LLMs: hallucination. If EKBM proves widely applicable, it could significantly improve the trustworthiness and usability of LLMs, particularly in error-sensitive applications. The proposed approach offers a practical trade-off between accuracy, computational cost, and helpfulness, which is crucial for real-world deployment.

*   **Strengths:**

    *   Addresses a core problem in LLM deployment (hallucination).
    *   Provides a practical framework for improving reliability.
    *   Integrates confidence estimation and a refinement model for a balanced approach.
    *   The "sure" and "unsure" output classification is a useful concept.
    *   The EKBM framework shows improvements on dialog state tracking tasks.
    *   Analysis of different DPO preference weighting strategies demonstrates the framework's flexibility.

*   **Weaknesses:**

    *   The reliance on OpenAI's GPT-4 for generating the reasoning chains could be problematic for reproducibility and cost.
    *   The experimental evaluation is limited to dialogue state tracking. Broader validation across different domains is necessary.
    *   The paper relies on multi-sampling to generate its SFT data. This leads to a more conservative view of the model's boundaries.
    *   The "slow reasoning" may come as a delay and potentially higher cost in real world deployments.

*   **Potential Influence:** The paper presents a scalable approach for enhancing LLM reliability and balancing accuracy with utility in error-sensitive applications, potentially influencing the development of more robust and trustworthy LLMs.

*   **Overall Assessment:** The paper makes a solid contribution by proposing a well-structured framework for improving LLM reliability. The integration of different components and the introduction of "sure" and "unsure" categories provide a practical approach to addressing hallucination. However, the reliance on GPT-4 for data generation and the limited scope of evaluation temper its significance.

**Score: 7.5**

**Rationale:**

The EKBM framework is well-motivated and offers a practical solution to a significant problem. The hybrid approach of confidence labeling and refinement is a strong point. The experimental results, though limited to DST, are promising. However, the reliance on GPT-4 for generating reasoning steps and the limited validation domain prevents a higher score. The proposed approach adds to the existing body of knowledge but does not represent a breakthrough. The evaluation in terms of cost (runtime/processing) could be more clearly stated to enhance significance.

- **Classification**: cs.CL
- **Score**: 7/10

### Haste Makes Waste: Evaluating Planning Abilities of LLMs for Efficient and Feasible Multitasking with Time Constraints Between Actions
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2503.02238v1)
- **Authors**: Zirui Wu, Xiao Liu, Jiayi Li, Lingpeng Kong, Yansong Feng
- **Abstract**: While Large Language Model-based agents have demonstrated substantial progress in task completion, existing evaluation benchmarks tend to overemphasize single-task performance, with insufficient attention given to the crucial aspects of multitask planning and execution efficiency required in real-world scenarios. To bridge this gap, we present Recipe2Plan, a novel benchmark framework based on real-world cooking scenarios. Unlike conventional benchmarks, Recipe2Plan challenges agents to optimize cooking time through parallel task execution while respecting temporal constraints i.e. specific actions need to be performed within a particular time intervals following the preceding steps. Overly aggressive local parallelization may disrupt this constraint, potentially compromising the entire cooking process. This strict time constraint between actions raises a unique challenge for agents to balance between maximizing concurrent operations and adhering to critical timing constraints. Extensive experiments with state-of-the-art models reveal challenges in maintaining this balance between efficiency and feasibility. The results highlight the need for improved temporal awareness and global multitasking capabilities in large language models. We open-source our benchmark and code at https://github.com/WilliamZR/Recipe2Plan.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the paper:

**Summary:**

The paper "Haste Makes Waste: Evaluating Planning Abilities of LLMs for Efficient and Feasible Multitasking with Time Constraints Between Actions" introduces RECIPE2PLAN, a new benchmark for evaluating the ability of Large Language Model (LLM)-based agents to perform efficient and feasible multitasking in realistic, time-constrained environments.  RECIPE2PLAN uses real-world cooking scenarios where agents must optimize cooking time by parallelizing tasks while respecting temporal constraints, i.e., specific actions needing to be performed within specific time intervals. The benchmark challenges agents to balance efficiency (minimizing total cooking time) with feasibility (adhering to time constraints).  Experiments with various LLMs reveal difficulties in maintaining this balance, highlighting the need for improved temporal awareness and global multitasking capabilities in LLMs. The paper open-sources the benchmark for further research.

**Critical Evaluation:**

* **Novelty:** The core novelty lies in the introduction of time constraints between actions as a critical component of evaluating LLM-based agents in multitasking scenarios.  Existing benchmarks often focus solely on task completion (feasibility) or efficiency in simplified environments without such temporal dependencies. Framing the evaluation as a *trade-off* between efficiency and feasibility is also valuable.  The idea of evaluating "haste makes waste" captures the essence of the problem well. The focus on real-world cooking recipes provides a relatable and intuitive setting. This isn't entirely new, as scheduling tasks have been proposed before, but the addition of real world constrains and the comparison to prior multitasking approaches makes this a relevant contribution.
* **Significance:** The significance stems from the increased relevance to real-world applications. In many domains (robotics, manufacturing, scientific experiments, even daily life), agents need to manage multiple tasks concurrently while respecting timing constraints. This work highlights a critical limitation of current LLMs in this area. The detailed error analysis and dissection of failure modes (global planning vs. local planning vs. common sense reasoning) are particularly valuable for guiding future research. While cooking may seem like a simple task, the application of these findings to more complex automation and planning processes would be a considerable step forward.

**Strengths:**

*   **Clearly Defined Problem:** The paper clearly articulates the problem of balancing efficiency and feasibility in time-constrained multitasking and demonstrates why existing benchmarks fall short.
*   **Realistic Benchmark:** The RECIPE2PLAN benchmark is grounded in real-world cooking scenarios, making it relatable and intuitive. The detailed description of properties like Action Concurrency and Dependencies are well thought out.
*   **Comprehensive Evaluation:** The authors conduct a thorough evaluation of several LLMs, including both open-source and closed-source models.
*   **Error Analysis:** The paper includes a valuable error analysis that identifies global planning as a key bottleneck. The breakdown of invalid action types is also very helpful. The controlled setup isolating each contributing factor through ablation studies and the oracle setting is well-executed.

**Weaknesses:**

*   **Limited Complexity:** While the cooking domain provides an intuitive setting, it might be argued that the complexity of the tasks (compared to, say, planning a multi-robot system in a warehouse) is relatively low. It is uncertain whether the conclusions will generalize to much more complex problems.
*   **Reliance on Text-Based Environment:** The agent's interaction is still limited to a text-based environment.  Integrating physical interaction (as suggested in the limitations) would significantly increase the complexity and relevance to embodied agents.
*   **Heuristic Baseline:** While the heuristic baseline is useful, its sub-optimality means that some agents may get penalized. A stronger baseline based on a more complete, but more expensive, search may strengthen the findings.
*   **Missing ablations:** It would be interesting to ablate the number of parallel recipes provided to the agents to test the impact of complexity on multitasking ability.

**Potential Influence:**

The paper has the potential to influence the development of more sophisticated LLM-based agents that can handle real-world tasks requiring temporal reasoning and multitasking.  RECIPE2PLAN can serve as a valuable benchmark for the community, driving research into more efficient and feasible planning algorithms.

**Justification for Score:**

The paper presents a novel and well-executed benchmark that exposes a critical limitation in current LLMs. The focus on time-constrained multitasking in a realistic setting fills a gap in existing evaluation frameworks. While the complexity of the cooking domain could be viewed as a limitation, the detailed error analysis and insightful results provide valuable guidance for future research. Its readily available dataset and code should encourage follow-up work, cementing its value in the field. The lack of comparisons to more advanced optimization methods beyond the heuristic could be strengthened.
Score: 7.5

- **Classification**: cs.CL
- **Score**: 7/10

### V2X-LLM: Enhancing V2X Integration and Understanding in Connected Vehicle Corridors
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2503.02239v1)
- **Authors**: Keshu Wu, Pei Li, Yang Zhou, Rui Gan, Junwei You, Yang Cheng, Jingwen Zhu, Steven T. Parker, Bin Ran, David A. Noyce, Zhengzhong Tu
- **Abstract**: The advancement of Connected and Automated Vehicles (CAVs) and Vehicle-to-Everything (V2X) offers significant potential for enhancing transportation safety, mobility, and sustainability. However, the integration and analysis of the diverse and voluminous V2X data, including Basic Safety Messages (BSMs) and Signal Phase and Timing (SPaT) data, present substantial challenges, especially on Connected Vehicle Corridors. These challenges include managing large data volumes, ensuring real-time data integration, and understanding complex traffic scenarios. Although these projects have developed an advanced CAV data pipeline that enables real-time communication between vehicles, infrastructure, and other road users for managing connected vehicle and roadside unit (RSU) data, significant hurdles in data comprehension and real-time scenario analysis and reasoning persist. To address these issues, we introduce the V2X-LLM framework, a novel enhancement to the existing CV data pipeline. V2X-LLM leverages Large Language Models (LLMs) to improve the understanding and real-time analysis of V2X data. The framework includes four key tasks: Scenario Explanation, offering detailed narratives of traffic conditions; V2X Data Description, detailing vehicle and infrastructure statuses; State Prediction, forecasting future traffic states; and Navigation Advisory, providing optimized routing instructions. By integrating LLM-driven reasoning with V2X data within the data pipeline, the V2X-LLM framework offers real-time feedback and decision support for traffic management. This integration enhances the accuracy of traffic analysis, safety, and traffic optimization. Demonstrations in a real-world urban corridor highlight the framework's potential to advance intelligent transportation systems.
- **Summary**: **Summary:** The paper titled "V2X-LLM: Enhancing V2X Integration and Understanding in Connected Vehicle Corridors" addresses the challenges associated with integrating and analyzing the extensive V2X data inherent in Connected and Automated Vehicles (CAVs). It emphasizes the need for improved real-time data integration related to Basic Safety Messages (BSMs) and Signal Phase and Timing (SPaT) information, especially in connected vehicle corridors. The authors propose the V2X-LLM framework, which utilizes Large Language Models (LLMs) to enhance the comprehension and analysis of V2X data. The framework performs four primary functions: Scenario Explanation, V2X Data Description, State Prediction, and Navigation Advisory, providing enhanced decision support for traffic management. Real-world demonstrations indicate that this framework has the potential to improve traffic safety and optimization in urban environments. **Critical Evaluation:** The novelty of the V2X-LLM framework lies in its application of Large Language Models to the realm of V2X data analysis, which is an emerging area of research in intelligent transportation systems. The integration of LLMs signifies a progressive shift towards utilizing advanced AI for real-time data processing and decision-making in traffic scenarios, a concept that has not been widely explored in existing literature. Strengths: 1. **Innovative Approach**: Using LLMs for traffic management is a fresh perspective that combines AI and transportation, addressing a significant gap in current methodologies. 2. **Real-time Applications**: The framework’s focus on real-time feedback and decision support enhances its practical applicability in managing contemporary traffic systems. 3. **Comprehensive Functionality**: It tackles multiple aspects of traffic management with interconnected tasks, potentially improving overall traffic flow and safety. Weaknesses: 1. **Generalizability**: The effectiveness of V2X-LLM in varied traffic scenarios or its robustness across different urban environments remains to be established. The paper mentions demonstrations in a single urban corridor, which may limit the overarching applicability of the findings. 2. **Complexity of Implementation**: The integration of LLMs into existing data pipelines may introduce additional complexities and challenges, which the paper does not extensively address. 3. **Dependence on Quality Data**: The success of the V2X-LLM framework is contingent on the quality and reliability of the V2X data, which may vary significantly across different locations and conditions. Overall, while the paper presents a significant augmentation of the CAV data pipeline, there are inherent challenges in terms of broader applicability and implementation that need to be explored further. Nonetheless, its innovative use of LLMs in traffic management could inspire future research directions and advancements in intelligent transportation systems. **Score: 8**
- **Classification**: cs.AI
- **Score**: 8/10

### OmniSQL: Synthesizing High-quality Text-to-SQL Data at Scale
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2503.02240v1)
- **Authors**: Haoyang Li, Shang Wu, Xiaokang Zhang, Xinmei Huang, Jing Zhang, Fuxin Jiang, Shuai Wang, Tieying Zhang, Jianjun Chen, Rui Shi, Hong Chen, Cuiping Li
- **Abstract**: Text-to-SQL, the task of translating natural language questions into SQL queries, plays a crucial role in enabling non-experts to interact with databases. While recent advancements in large language models (LLMs) have significantly enhanced text-to-SQL performance, existing approaches face notable limitations in real-world text-to-SQL applications. Prompting-based methods often depend on closed-source LLMs, which are expensive, raise privacy concerns, and lack customization. Fine-tuning-based methods, on the other hand, suffer from poor generalizability due to the limited coverage of publicly available training data. To overcome these challenges, we propose a novel and scalable text-to-SQL data synthesis framework for automatically synthesizing large-scale, high-quality, and diverse datasets without extensive human intervention. Using this framework, we introduce SynSQL-2.5M, the first million-scale text-to-SQL dataset, containing 2.5 million samples spanning over 16,000 synthetic databases. Each sample includes a database, SQL query, natural language question, and chain-of-thought (CoT) solution. Leveraging SynSQL-2.5M, we develop OmniSQL, a powerful open-source text-to-SQL model available in three sizes: 7B, 14B, and 32B. Extensive evaluations across nine datasets demonstrate that OmniSQL achieves state-of-the-art performance, matching or surpassing leading closed-source and open-source LLMs, including GPT-4o and DeepSeek-V3, despite its smaller size. We release all code, datasets, and models to support further research.
- **Summary**: ### Summary of the Paper The paper presents a novel framework for synthesizing high-quality text-to-SQL data, addressing significant limitations faced by current methods in translating natural language to SQL queries. The authors highlight the drawbacks of existing prompting-based and fine-tuning-based approaches, such as dependency on costly closed-source models and poor generalizability due to limited datasets. To tackle these challenges, they developed a scalable framework resulting in the SynSQL-2.5M dataset, which consists of 2.5 million samples derived from over 16,000 synthetic databases. This dataset includes SQL queries, natural language questions, and chain-of-thought solutions. The authors also introduce OmniSQL, an open-source text-to-SQL model available in three sizes, which demonstrates state-of-the-art performance against both closed and open-source models, even outperforming some larger models despite its smaller size. The paper makes all resources available for further research. ### Rigorous and Critical Evaluation **Novelty**: The approach is innovative in its synthesis of a large-scale dataset for text-to-SQL tasks, especially with its focus on mitigating the privacy and customization issues associated with closed-source models. The creation of SynSQL-2.5M serves as a significant contribution, potentially filling the gap left by the limited public datasets previously available. Additionally, the introduction of OmniSQL as an open-source model encourages accessibility in the research community. **Significance**: The implications of this work are substantial, as improving text-to-SQL systems can greatly enhance how non-experts interact with databases. The authors' methodology may set a precedent for future work in automated data generation and model training in this space. The release of code and data encourages reproducibility and community engagement, which are critical for further advancements. **Strengths**: - Significant dataset with diverse examples contributes to improved generalizability. - Open-source model availability fosters collaboration and development by the research community. - State-of-the-art performance demonstrated, indicating practical viability over existing solutions. **Weaknesses**: - The reliance on synthetic data may lead to concerns regarding the relevance of results in real-world applications, as synthetic datasets might not fully capture the complexity of genuine user queries. - While the paper demonstrates superiority in performance, deeper evaluations in varied practical settings are necessary to ascertain broad applicability and robustness. - The scaling aspect is admirable, yet further analysis on computational requirements and environmental impact resulting from training large models may be warranted. **Conclusion**: Given the originality of the data synthesis approach, substantial contributions to dataset creation, and the development of a competitive open-source model, the paper represents a meaningful step forward in the text-to-SQL paradigm. **Score: 8**  This score reflects the paper’s notable contribution to the field, marked by its innovative approach and comprehensive resources. However, it also acknowledges the need for validation in real-world applications and considerations of the impacts of synthetic datasets. Overall, the work holds strong promise for advancing the text-to-SQL landscape, meriting high commendation but leaving scope for further exploration.
- **Classification**: cs.CL
- **Score**: 8/10

### $\mathbfΦ$-GAN: Physics-Inspired GAN for Generating SAR Images Under Limited Data
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2503.02242v1)
- **Authors**: Xidan Zhang, Yihan Zhuang, Qian Guo, Haodong Yang, Xuelin Qian, Gong Cheng, Junwei Han, Zhongling Huang
- **Abstract**: Approaches for improving generative adversarial networks (GANs) training under a few samples have been explored for natural images. However, these methods have limited effectiveness for synthetic aperture radar (SAR) images, as they do not account for the unique electromagnetic scattering properties of SAR. To remedy this, we propose a physics-inspired regularization method dubbed $\Phi$-GAN, which incorporates the ideal point scattering center (PSC) model of SAR with two physical consistency losses. The PSC model approximates SAR targets using physical parameters, ensuring that $\Phi$-GAN generates SAR images consistent with real physical properties while preventing discriminator overfitting by focusing on PSC-based decision cues. To embed the PSC model into GANs for end-to-end training, we introduce a physics-inspired neural module capable of estimating the physical parameters of SAR targets efficiently. This module retains the interpretability of the physical model and can be trained with limited data. We propose two physical loss functions: one for the generator, guiding it to produce SAR images with physical parameters consistent with real ones, and one for the discriminator, enhancing its robustness by basing decisions on PSC attributes. We evaluate $\Phi$-GAN across several conditional GAN (cGAN) models, demonstrating state-of-the-art performance in data-scarce scenarios on three SAR image datasets.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the paper:

**Summary:**

The paper introduces Φ-GAN, a physics-inspired generative adversarial network designed to generate Synthetic Aperture Radar (SAR) images, particularly in data-scarce scenarios. The core idea is to incorporate an ideal point scattering center (PSC) model of SAR into the GAN framework.  A physics-inspired neural module is used to estimate physical parameters of SAR targets efficiently, and two physical loss functions are introduced: one to guide the generator towards producing SAR images with consistent physical parameters, and another to enhance the discriminator's robustness by basing decisions on PSC attributes. The method is tested on three SAR image datasets, showing state-of-the-art performance.

**Critical Evaluation:**

**Novelty:**

*   **Integration of Physical Model:** Integrating a physical model (PSC) directly into the GAN training process for SAR image generation is a significant novelty. While previous works have explored incorporating physical principles, this paper presents an end-to-end, trainable framework.  It's particularly innovative how they've created a differentiable neural module to estimate PSC parameters, making the whole process trainable.
*   **Dual-Discriminator with Physical Consistency Loss:** The use of a dual-discriminator (image-based and PSC-based) coupled with physical consistency losses is a well-designed approach to regularize the GAN and prevent discriminator overfitting in the face of limited data. This is a valuable contribution that differentiates it from standard regularization techniques.
*   **Unrolling HQS:** The use of an unrolled Half-Quadratic Splitting (HQS) method to estimate SAR parameters is a practical advancement. HQS makes the model more efficient and better-suited for training with limited data.

**Significance:**

*   **Addressing Data Scarcity in SAR:**  SAR image datasets are often limited due to annotation costs and the specific electromagnetic nature of SAR, making GAN training challenging. This work offers a practical solution by leveraging the PSC model, potentially unlocking applications previously limited by data availability.
*   **Improved SAR Image Generation:** The results demonstrate a clear improvement in the quality and physical consistency of generated SAR images compared to existing methods, especially under data-scarce conditions.  This directly impacts applications such as SAR image simulation, target recognition, and data augmentation for downstream tasks.
*   **Generalizability:** Showing that Φ-GAN is adaptable to various cGAN architectures increases its potential adoption and broader impact.

**Strengths:**

*   **Well-Defined Problem:**  The paper tackles a real and significant challenge in the SAR image processing community: generating realistic data with limited samples.
*   **Strong Technical Approach:** The proposed method is technically sound, combining GAN training with the PSC model in a novel and efficient manner.
*   **Clear and Comprehensive Experiments:** The experiments are well-designed, comparing Φ-GAN to several baselines and demonstrating its superior performance on multiple datasets. Ablation studies effectively show the contributions of each component of the proposed method.
*   **Physical Interpretability:** By integrating the PSC model, the generated SAR images are more physically realistic and interpretable. This could be advantageous in scenarios where understanding the underlying physical properties is essential.

**Weaknesses:**

*   **Complexity of PSC Model:** While the paper simplifies the integration of the PSC model, it may still be complex to implement and train.
*   **Specific to SAR:** The reliance on the PSC model might limit its direct applicability to other image generation tasks beyond SAR.
*   **Hyperparameter Sensitivity:**  GAN training is known for its sensitivity to hyperparameter choices.  Although implementation details are provided, more discussion about the sensitivity of Φ-GAN to hyperparameter settings would be valuable.
*   **Limited Diversity Analysis:** While the paper demonstrates improved image quality, a more thorough analysis of the diversity of generated images would further strengthen the claims of improved generalization.

**Potential Influence:**

Φ-GAN has the potential to significantly impact the SAR image processing community by providing a more effective and physically grounded approach to SAR image generation in data-scarce environments.  It can influence future research directions in applying physical models in deep learning and encourage the development of interpretable and robust GAN models for remote sensing applications.

**Score: 8**

**Justification:**

The paper offers a novel and technically strong approach to SAR image generation, effectively addressing the challenge of data scarcity. Integrating the PSC model into the GAN framework is a significant contribution. The method is well-evaluated, demonstrating improved performance and physical consistency of generated images. While the method might be somewhat complex to implement and specific to SAR, the benefits it provides in this specific domain are considerable. The paper represents a significant advance and is likely to have a notable impact on future research in the field. The weaknesses listed above prevent it from receiving a higher score.

- **Classification**: cs.CV
- **Score**: 8/10

### From Code to Courtroom: LLMs as the New Software Judges
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2503.02246v1)
- **Authors**: Junda He, Jieke Shi, Terry Yue Zhuo, Christoph Treude, Jiamou Sun, Zhenchang Xing, Xiaoning Du, David Lo
- **Abstract**: Recently, Large Language Models (LLMs) have been increasingly used to automate SE tasks such as code generation and summarization. However, evaluating the quality of LLM-generated software artifacts remains challenging. Human evaluation, while effective, is very costly and time-consuming. Traditional automated metrics like BLEU rely on high-quality references and struggle to capture nuanced aspects of software quality, such as readability and usefulness. In response, the LLM-as-a-Judge paradigm, which employs LLMs for automated evaluation, has emerged. Given that LLMs are typically trained to align with human judgment and possess strong coding abilities and reasoning skills, they hold promise as cost-effective and scalable surrogates for human evaluators. Nevertheless, LLM-as-a-Judge research in the SE community is still in its early stages, with many breakthroughs needed. This forward-looking SE 2030 paper aims to steer the research community toward advancing LLM-as-a-Judge for evaluating LLMgenerated software artifacts, while also sharing potential research paths to achieve this goal. We provide a literature review of existing SE studies on LLM-as-a-Judge and envision these frameworks as reliable, robust, and scalable human surrogates capable of evaluating software artifacts with consistent, multi-faceted assessments by 2030 and beyond. To validate this vision, we analyze the limitations of current studies, identify key research gaps, and outline a detailed roadmap to guide future developments of LLM-as-a-Judge in software engineering. While not intended to be a definitive guide, our work aims to foster further research and adoption of LLM-as-a-Judge frameworks within the SE community, ultimately improving the effectiveness and scalability of software artifact evaluation methods.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the provided research paper abstract and introduction:

**Summary:**

The paper "From Code to Courtroom: LLMs as the New Software Judges" addresses the challenge of evaluating software artifacts generated by Large Language Models (LLMs) in Software Engineering (SE).  It proposes using LLMs themselves ("LLM-as-a-Judge") as a cost-effective and scalable solution compared to human evaluation or traditional automated metrics.  The paper presents a literature review, outlines a vision for LLM-as-a-Judge systems by 2030, identifies research gaps, and suggests a roadmap for future development to improve the effectiveness and reliability of software artifact evaluation. It aims to guide the SE research community in this direction.

**Critical Evaluation:**

**Novelty:** The idea of LLMs evaluating other LLMs' outputs in SE is not entirely novel. While previous works have explored LLMs for specific SE tasks, this paper attempts to provide a broader vision and roadmap for the "LLM-as-a-Judge" paradigm, encompassing various software artifact evaluation scenarios. The paper itself recognizes existing studies. The primary novelty lies in the comprehensive vision, the systematization of existing work within the "LLM-as-a-Judge" framing, and the identification of specific research gaps and a forward-looking roadmap. However, the fundamental concept is built on existing research.

**Significance:** The problem addressed is undoubtedly significant. The increasing use of LLMs in SE necessitates efficient and reliable evaluation methods. If the proposed "LLM-as-a-Judge" approach can achieve human-level accuracy and consistency at a lower cost and higher scalability, it would be a substantial contribution. However, the paper's significance depends on how well the roadmap is articulated and how effectively it inspires the research community to address the identified challenges.  The recognition of the limitations of existing methods (human evaluation and traditional metrics) and the suggestion of LLMs as a potential solution is an important stepping stone, but the real value will be in the proposed solutions and their impact.

**Strengths:**

*   **Clearly defined problem:** The paper highlights a critical bottleneck in the LLM-assisted software development lifecycle.
*   **Comprehensive vision:** It paints a picture of a future where LLMs play a central role in evaluating software artifacts.
*   **Identified research gaps:** The paper identifies specific limitations in current approaches, providing concrete areas for future research.
*   **Structured roadmap:**  The roadmap provides actionable steps to guide research in the field.
*   **Well-written and articulated**: The paper is clearly written, setting the context and laying out the purpose of the study early on.

**Weaknesses:**

*   **Incremental Novelty:**  The foundational idea builds upon existing research. The novelty is mainly in the systematization and roadmap.
*   **Lack of Empirical Validation:** The paper primarily presents a vision and roadmap without strong empirical evidence to support the feasibility of the proposed approach.  It relies on the promise of LLMs based on existing capabilities, without deeply exploring the potential pitfalls.
*   **Over-reliance on LLMs:** The vision might be overly optimistic about LLMs' capabilities and potentially overlooks alternative approaches or hybrid methods.
*   **Bias concerns:** While mentioned, the potential for bias in LLM-as-a-Judge systems needs to be addressed in greater detail. The biases can stem from the data the models are trained on and potentially limit the generalization of the tool.

**Justification for the score:**

I am assigning a score of 6. The paper makes a valuable contribution by framing existing and future LLM-based evaluation techniques in the SE domain under the "LLM-as-a-Judge" paradigm and identifying key research directions to advance this paradigm. This provides a useful conceptual framework for the community. The roadmap helps to set a direction. However, the paper has some limitations. First, it is still very early in the development of the field and the paper may prove to be a prediction that doesn't come to fruition. Second, the paper focuses a lot on what needs to be done but doesn't provide many new insights and only briefly touches on potential limitations. Finally, the lack of strong empirical validation of the proposed vision limits its immediate impact. More rigorous experimentation and benchmarking will be necessary to demonstrate the real-world effectiveness and address the concerns that are not currently addressed.

Score: 6

- **Classification**: cs.SE
- **Score**: 6/10

### Making Better Mistakes in CLIP-Based Zero-Shot Classification with Hierarchy-Aware Language Prompts
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2503.02248v1)
- **Authors**: Tong Liang, Jim Davis
- **Abstract**: Recent studies are leveraging advancements in large language models (LLMs) trained on extensive internet-crawled text data to generate textual descriptions of downstream classes in CLIP-based zero-shot image classification. While most of these approaches aim at improving accuracy, our work focuses on ``making better mistakes", of which the mistakes' severities are derived from the given label hierarchy of downstream tasks. Since CLIP's image encoder is trained with language supervising signals, it implicitly captures the hierarchical semantic relationships between different classes. This motivates our goal of making better mistakes in zero-shot classification, a task for which CLIP is naturally well-suited. Our approach (HAPrompts) queries the language model to produce textual representations for given classes as zero-shot classifiers of CLIP to perform image classification on downstream tasks. To our knowledge, this is the first work to introduce making better mistakes in CLIP-based zero-shot classification. Our approach outperforms the related methods in a holistic comparison across five datasets of varying scales with label hierarchies of different heights in our experiments. Our code and LLM-generated image prompts: \href{https://github.com/ltong1130ztr/HAPrompts}{https://github.com/ltong1130ztr/HAPrompts}.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the paper:

**Summary:**

The paper proposes a novel approach (HAPrompts) to improve the performance of CLIP-based zero-shot image classification by focusing on "making better mistakes". Instead of simply maximizing accuracy, the method aims to reduce the severity of errors, where severity is determined by the label hierarchy of the dataset. HAPrompts leverages Large Language Models (LLMs) to generate textual representations (image prompts) of classes, incorporating hierarchical relationships.  The approach uses comparative and path-based prompts to guide the LLM to generate more informed textual representations of the classes, emphasizing differences between similar classes in the hierarchy.  The method is training-free and dataset-agnostic, outperforming related methods across several benchmark datasets with varying scales and label hierarchy depths.

**Critical Evaluation:**

*   **Novelty:** The paper introduces the concept of explicitly focusing on "making better mistakes" in the context of CLIP-based zero-shot classification. While previous works have used label hierarchies, the explicit goal of reducing error severity *during inference* (as opposed to during training or architecture design) with *prompt engineering* is a key difference.  Using hierarchical information to generate more effective language prompts is a sensible and practical idea. While LLM prompting for zero-shot has been explored, the specific prompting strategies (comparative and path-based) tailored for hierarchical label structures add a layer of novelty.

*   **Significance:** The results demonstrate improvements across multiple datasets compared to competitive baselines, suggesting the method is effective and generalizable within the domain of CLIP-based zero-shot classification. The training-free nature is a significant advantage, as it allows for easy integration into existing workflows. The dataset-agnostic design makes it practical for a range of applications without requiring task-specific prompt engineering. The demonstration of better HD@1 scores is very relevant to real-world use cases of these models.

*   **Strengths:**

    *   **Clear Problem Definition:**  The paper clearly articulates the problem of error severity in zero-shot learning and motivates the proposed solution effectively.
    *   **Well-Designed Approach:** HAPrompts is a well-designed method with clear and intuitive language prompts that leverage hierarchical information.
    *   **Strong Empirical Results:** The results on multiple datasets show a consistent improvement over baselines in terms of accuracy and error severity.
    *   **Training-Free and Dataset-Agnostic:** These features make the approach practical and easy to use.
    *   **Ablation study** shows a useful analysis of the proposed methodology.
    *   **Demonstrates robustness of the solution by providing results on various LLMs.**
    *   **Clear explanation of the method.**

*   **Weaknesses:**

    *   **Dependency on LLM Quality:** Like all LLM-prompting techniques, the method's performance heavily relies on the quality of the LLM. It is possible results could have been substantially different if using a different LLM.
    *   **Limited Error Analysis:** While histograms are given in Appendix 7.6, the error examples provided in Figures 12 and 13 are minimal and provide limited qualitative insights into the specific types of errors the method still makes. A deeper error analysis could provide avenues for further improvement.
    *   **Computational Resources:** While the approach itself is training-free, the LLM prompting process can still be computationally expensive, especially for large datasets. The paper doesn't address the inference time or computational costs.

*   **Potential Influence:** The paper can influence future research on zero-shot learning by highlighting the importance of error severity and exploring the potential of label hierarchies for improving classification performance. The proposed prompting techniques could be adapted for other tasks and datasets.

**Justification for Score:**

Considering the novelty and significance, but also keeping in mind the weaknesses discussed above, the paper demonstrates a clear contribution to the field. It's not a complete paradigm shift but a solid improvement on existing methods. The benefits and limitations are well presented and reasonably explained. The thorough evaluation also adds to the paper's value.

Score: 7

- **Classification**: cs.CV
- **Score**: 7/10

### Large Language Models as Natural Selector for Embodied Soft Robot Design
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2503.02249v1)
- **Authors**: Changhe Chen, Xiaohao Xu, Xiangdong Wang, Xiaonan Huang
- **Abstract**: Designing soft robots is a complex and iterative process that demands cross-disciplinary expertise in materials science, mechanics, and control, often relying on intuition and extensive experimentation. While Large Language Models (LLMs) have demonstrated impressive reasoning abilities, their capacity to learn and apply embodied design principles--crucial for creating functional robotic systems--remains largely unexplored. This paper introduces RoboCrafter-QA, a novel benchmark to evaluate whether LLMs can learn representations of soft robot designs that effectively bridge the gap between high-level task descriptions and low-level morphological and material choices. RoboCrafter-QA leverages the EvoGym simulator to generate a diverse set of soft robot design challenges, spanning robotic locomotion, manipulation, and balancing tasks. Our experiments with state-of-the-art multi-modal LLMs reveal that while these models exhibit promising capabilities in learning design representations, they struggle with fine-grained distinctions between designs with subtle performance differences. We further demonstrate the practical utility of LLMs for robot design initialization. Our code and benchmark will be available to encourage the community to foster this exciting research direction.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the paper "Large Language Models as Natural Selector for Embodied Soft Robot Design":

**Summary:**

The paper introduces RoboCrafter-QA, a novel benchmark designed to evaluate the embodied design reasoning capabilities of multi-modal Large Language Models (LLMs) in the context of soft robot design. The benchmark utilizes the EvoGym simulator to generate a diverse Question-Answering (QA) dataset relating robot morphologies, task descriptions, and environmental constraints.  The authors conduct experiments with state-of-the-art LLMs, analyzing their performance on robot design selection tasks. They reveal that while LLMs show promise in understanding design representations, they struggle with fine-grained distinctions between designs with subtle performance differences. The paper also demonstrates the potential of LLMs for robot design initialization by leveraging reference designs.

**Critical Evaluation:**

*   **Novelty:** The paper's novelty lies in its explicit focus on evaluating *embodied design reasoning* in LLMs for *soft robot design*, a relatively unexplored area. Creating a benchmark specifically tailored to this domain, especially one based on a physics simulator like EvoGym, is a valuable contribution. While existing LLM benchmarks test general reasoning or scientific understanding, RoboCrafter-QA directly addresses the complexities of physical system interactions and morphology-performance relationships, which is a distinct and important contribution.
*   **Significance:** The significance of the paper is threefold:
    *   **Benchmark Development:**  RoboCrafter-QA fills a gap in the LLM evaluation landscape by providing a platform to assess the ability of LLMs to reason about physical systems and morphology in robotic design. This is vital for developing AI-driven robotic design tools.
    *   **Performance Analysis:** The experiments provide crucial insights into the capabilities and limitations of current LLMs in the domain of embodied soft robot design. The finding that LLMs struggle with subtle design differences points towards areas needing improvement in representation learning and reasoning.
    *   **Potential Applications:** Demonstrating that LLMs can initialize designs with reference knowledge suggests a practical application for using LLMs to accelerate and improve the robot design process.
*   **Strengths:**
    *   **Well-Defined Benchmark:**  RoboCrafter-QA is clearly defined, well-motivated, and uses a established robotics simulator (EvoGym) as ground truth.
    *   **Comprehensive Evaluation:** The evaluation uses multiple metrics (accuracy, consistency, DWA) and explores performance across a diverse set of environments and task difficulties.
    *   **Ablation Study:** The ablation study regarding prompt design provides valuable insight on what components of the prompt matters most.
    *   **Practical Application:**  The design initialization experiment highlights the potential to translate benchmark results into real-world applications.
*   **Weaknesses:**
    *   **Limited Design Space:** The 5x5 voxel grid represents a simplified design space. Although this simplifies the problem and makes evaluation feasible, it limits the complexity and realism of the designs. This needs to be discussed more. The grid size also impacts scalability of the proposed method.
    *   **Sim-to-Real Gap:** The study is conducted entirely in simulation. The paper mentions the sim-to-real gap, but does not address it or suggest possible ways for closing it. It's a significant limitation, as the performance of LLM-designed robots in the real world is the ultimate goal.
    *   **Scope of Initialization:** While the paper shows that LLMs can perform well for initialization, there is no indication of how this initialization can be used in larger robot development pipelines.

*   **Potential Influence:**  The RoboCrafter-QA benchmark has the potential to become a valuable resource for the robotics and AI communities. It can drive research on improving LLMs' embodied design reasoning abilities, and can inform the development of more sophisticated AI-driven robotic design tools. However, the impact will depend on the community's adoption and further development of the benchmark.

**Justification for Score:**

The paper presents a significant contribution by creating a novel benchmark for evaluating LLMs in the context of soft robot design. The experiments provide valuable insights into the capabilities and limitations of current LLMs, and the demonstrated potential for design initialization is promising. The framework is well-defined and experiments are well-designed. However, the limitations regarding the simplified design space and the sim-to-real gap are significant and cannot be ignored. While RoboCrafter-QA addresses a specific gap in the benchmarking of AI for robotics, it is an incremental contribution that, whilst significant, does not fundamentally reshape the field in its current form.

**Score: 7**

- **Classification**: cs.RO
- **Score**: 7/10

### AppAgentX: Evolving GUI Agents as Proficient Smartphone Users
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2503.02268v1)
- **Authors**: Wenjia Jiang, Yangyang Zhuang, Chenxi Song, Xu Yang, Chi Zhang
- **Abstract**: Recent advancements in Large Language Models (LLMs) have led to the development of intelligent LLM-based agents capable of interacting with graphical user interfaces (GUIs). These agents demonstrate strong reasoning and adaptability, enabling them to perform complex tasks that traditionally required predefined rules. However, the reliance on step-by-step reasoning in LLM-based agents often results in inefficiencies, particularly for routine tasks. In contrast, traditional rule-based systems excel in efficiency but lack the intelligence and flexibility to adapt to novel scenarios. To address this challenge, we propose a novel evolutionary framework for GUI agents that enhances operational efficiency while retaining intelligence and flexibility. Our approach incorporates a memory mechanism that records the agent's task execution history. By analyzing this history, the agent identifies repetitive action sequences and evolves high-level actions that act as shortcuts, replacing these low-level operations and improving efficiency. This allows the agent to focus on tasks requiring more complex reasoning, while simplifying routine actions. Experimental results on multiple benchmark tasks demonstrate that our approach significantly outperforms existing methods in both efficiency and accuracy. The code will be open-sourced to support further research.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the paper:

**Summary:**

The paper "AppAgentX: Evolving GUI Agents as Proficient Smartphone Users" introduces a novel framework for GUI agents designed to enhance their operational efficiency and intelligence. The core idea revolves around an evolutionary mechanism where the agent learns from its task execution history, identifying repetitive action sequences and abstracting them into high-level "shortcut" actions. This evolution allows the agent to bypass inefficient, low-level operations, focusing its reasoning capabilities on more complex tasks. The framework includes a memory mechanism to record task execution history and a chain-based knowledge base for behavior optimization. Experimental results demonstrate improved efficiency and accuracy compared to existing methods. The approach is visually grounded, avoiding the need for backend APIs.

**Critical Evaluation:**

*   **Novelty:** The idea of evolving GUI agents by abstracting repetitive action sequences is incremental.  While existing LLM-based agents excel at complex reasoning and adaptability, they often suffer from inefficiencies in routine tasks. AppAgentX tackles this by incorporating a memory mechanism to record task execution history and an evolutionary mechanism to create high-level "shortcut" actions. A novel evolutionary framework for GUI agents is developed in order to improve agent efficiency and performance. However, the individual components—LLMs, memory, action abstraction—have been explored previously. The primary novelty lies in the combination and application within the GUI agent context.  The claim of novelty would be stronger with more detailed comparisons and ablation studies with existing agents that also employ some memory and/or abstraction techniques.
*   **Significance:**  The paper addresses a critical practical challenge in deploying LLM-based GUI agents: the trade-off between intelligence and efficiency. By enabling agents to learn and adapt their actions over time, the framework has the potential to improve the user experience and reduce the computational cost of running these agents. The significance is demonstrated by empirical evaluations on several benchmark tasks which show gains in execution time, token consumption, and success rate. If successfully generalized, the approach can have impact by creating faster and more capable GUI agents. However, this depends on its scalability and adaptability to a diverse range of application domains.
*   **Strengths:**
    *   The paper clearly articulates the problem of inefficiency in routine tasks for LLM-based GUI agents.
    *   The proposed evolutionary framework is well-defined and the components are clearly described.
    *   The experimental results demonstrate the effectiveness of the approach across several benchmarks.
    *   The paper includes an ablation study to analyze the contribution of different components.
*   **Weaknesses:**
    *   The novelty is somewhat limited as it builds upon existing concepts in LLM-based agents, memory, and abstraction. More detailed comparison with existing work is needed.
    *   While the experiments demonstrate improved performance on specific benchmarks, the generalizability of the approach to other GUI environments and task domains needs further investigation.
    *   The paper could benefit from a more in-depth analysis of the limitations of the approach, such as the potential for overfitting to specific task sequences or the computational cost of the evolutionary process.

**Justification of Score:**

The paper presents a sound and practical approach to improving the efficiency of LLM-based GUI agents. While the novelty is incremental, the combination of existing techniques and the clear experimental validation warrant a positive evaluation. The results are promising and suggest a potential for further research and development in this area. A score of 7 reflects the balance between the incremental novelty, the potential significance of the results, and the identified weaknesses regarding generalizability and more rigorous comparison to existing approaches.

**Score: 7**
- **Classification**: cs.AI
- **Score**: 7/10

### Memorize or Generalize? Evaluating LLM Code Generation with Evolved Questions
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2503.02296v1)
- **Authors**: Wentao Chen, Lizhe Zhang, Li Zhong, Letian Peng, Zilong Wang, Jingbo Shang
- **Abstract**: Large Language Models (LLMs) are known to exhibit a memorization phenomenon in code generation: instead of truly understanding the underlying principles of a programming problem, they tend to memorize the original prompt and its solution together in the training. Consequently, when facing variants of the original problem, their answers very likely resemble the memorized solutions and fail to generalize. In this paper, we investigate this phenomenon by designing three evolution strategies to create variants: mutation, paraphrasing, and code-rewriting. By comparing the performance and AST similarity of the LLM-generated codes before and after these three evolutions, we develop a memorization score that positively correlates with the level of memorization. As expected, as supervised fine-tuning goes on, the memorization score rises before overfitting, suggesting more severe memorization. We demonstrate that common mitigation approaches, such as prompt translation and using evolved variants as data augmentation in supervised learning and reinforcement learning, either compromise the performance or fail to alleviate the memorization issue. Therefore, memorization remains a significant challenge in LLM code generation, highlighting the need for a more effective solution.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the paper:

**Summary:**

The paper investigates the memorization phenomenon in LLMs for code generation, proposing a multi-level evolution framework (mutation, paraphrasing, and code-rewriting) to evaluate how LLMs handle variations of coding tasks.  It introduces a "memorization score" that combines functional correctness and AST similarity to quantify early-stage memorization. The authors demonstrate that code-specialized LLMs exhibit high memorization, and common mitigation techniques (supervised fine-tuning, reinforcement learning, and problem translation) often come at the cost of performance on original tasks. They conclude that memorization remains a significant challenge in LLM code generation.

**Critical Evaluation:**

* **Novelty:** The paper makes a contribution by proposing a multi-level evolution framework that allows for detailed analysis of the LLM's behaviour when facing different types of prompt variations. The introduction of a novel "memorization score" is another significant addition. While the general phenomenon of memorization in LLMs is well-known, its nuanced characterization within the code generation context, along with quantitative measure, offers some incremental novelty.  However, existing works have also touched on similar ideas of code evolution (e.g., EvalPlus) to reduce memorization biases and measure generalization abilities.
* **Significance:** The significance of the work lies in its attempt to provide a more structured understanding of memorization, going beyond simply observing performance drops on slightly altered tasks. The memorization score attempts to quantify this phenomenon, which could be valuable for future research into more effective mitigation strategies. The investigation of three mitigation methods helps to identify tradeoffs between memorization and general performance. The case study provides a clear illustrative example of the memorization phenomenon at work, providing intuition behind the quantitative results.
* **Strengths:**
    * **Comprehensive Evaluation Framework:**  The multi-level evolution approach is well-motivated and allows for dissecting different aspects of memorization (textual noise, semantic variation, code structure alterations).
    * **Quantifiable Metric:** The "memorization score" provides a concrete way to measure and compare the extent of memorization across different models and training regimes.
    * **Practical Mitigation Investigation:**  The evaluation of mitigation strategies gives valuable insights into the challenges of addressing memorization in practice.
    * **Focus on Early-Stage Memorization:** By focusing on the early stages before overfitting, the paper highlights a distinct phenomenon from standard overfitting behaviour.
* **Weaknesses:**
    * **Limited Scope of Datasets:**  The study relies on a relatively simple dataset (MBPP-Plus). The findings might not generalize directly to more complex coding tasks.
    * **Simplistic Mitigation Strategies:**  The explored mitigation methods are standard techniques (fine-tuning, RL, translation). It does not investigate novel mitigation strategy.
    * **Memorization Score limitations:** While the memorization score is novel, its effectiveness might be limited by its reliance on accuracy, which may not always reflect true understanding. AST similarity also has its limitations.  It's unclear if the weighting of different components within the memorization score is optimal.
    * **LLM choice Limitations**: Evaluating only two LLMs (Qwen2.5 and Llama3.1) weakens the generality of conclusions.
* **Potential Influence:** The paper's findings could influence future research directions in LLM code generation.  The proposed evaluation framework and memorization score provide useful tools for assessing memorization and guiding the development of more robust training methods.  The observed trade-offs between memorization and performance could motivate the exploration of alternative training techniques that strike a better balance.
* **Overall Assessment:**  The paper makes a valuable contribution by providing a more structured and quantitative analysis of memorization in LLM code generation. However, the impact is somewhat limited by the simplicity of the evaluation tasks and mitigation strategies.

**Score: 6**

**Rationale:**  The paper presents a good analysis of a well-known issue but its contribution is more incremental than groundbreaking. The multi-level framework and memorization score offer a useful improvement over existing methods, allowing for more detailed analysis.  However, the limited dataset and lack of investigation of novel mitigation strategies decrease the impact.  While the results are interesting, the findings confirm what the code generation research already suspects.

**Rigorous Rationale**: The score reflects that the paper is a solid contribution, clearly written and well-executed, but does not introduce any radical or transformative ideas. The paper confirms the existing intuition, provides a more solid, quantified base, and a useful toolbox for further research but its value is more iterative than radical. Hence, score = 6.

- **Classification**: cs.AI
- **Score**: 6/10

### Towards Explainable Doctor Recommendation with Large Language Models
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2503.02298v1)
- **Authors**: Ziyang Zeng, Dongyuan Li, Yuqing Yang
- **Abstract**: The advent of internet medicine provides patients with unprecedented convenience in searching and communicating with doctors relevant to their diseases and desired treatments online. However, the current doctor recommendation systems fail to fully ensure the professionalism and interpretability of the recommended results. In this work, we formulate doctor recommendation as a ranking task and develop a large language model (LLM)-based pointwise ranking framework. Our framework ranks doctors according to their relevance regarding specific diseases-treatment pairs in a zero-shot setting. The advantage of our framework lies in its ability to generate precise and explainable doctor ranking results. Additionally, we construct DrRank, a new expertise-driven doctor ranking dataset comprising over 38 disease-treatment pairs. Experiment results on the DrRank dataset demonstrate that our framework significantly outperforms the strongest cross-encoder baseline, achieving a notable gain of +5.45 in the NDCG@10 score while maintaining affordable latency consumption. Furthermore, we comprehensively present the fairness analysis results of our framework from three perspectives of different diseases, patient gender, and geographical regions. Meanwhile, the interpretability of our framework is rigorously verified by three human experts, providing further evidence of the reliability of our proposed framework for doctor recommendation.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the paper:

**Summary:**

The paper addresses the problem of doctor recommendation, arguing that current systems lack professionalism and interpretability. The authors propose a pointwise ranking framework based on large language models (LLMs) to rank doctors by relevance to specific disease-treatment pairs in a zero-shot setting. A key contribution is the creation of a new dataset, DrRank, comprised of doctor profiles and expert annotations of relevance. The framework leverages LLMs to generate both rankings and explanations for recommendations. The paper includes extensive experiments demonstrating improved ranking accuracy over baseline cross-encoders and examines fairness along different axes (disease, gender, geography) and interpretability (assessed by human experts).

**Critical Evaluation:**

*   **Novelty:** The paper's novelty lies primarily in its application of LLMs to the doctor recommendation domain with a focus on explainability and the introduction of the DrRank dataset. While using LLMs for ranking isn't entirely new, its implementation in the medical field, paired with the generation of ranking criteria and evaluation rationales, adds a valuable layer of innovation. The creation of DrRank addresses a gap in available datasets suitable for this specific task.
*   **Significance:** The potential impact of the paper is significant, especially given the increasing reliance on online medical information. A more accurate, fair, and transparent doctor recommendation system could lead to better patient outcomes and increased trust in online healthcare platforms. The fairness analysis is a particularly important contribution, highlighting potential biases that need to be addressed in real-world deployments. However, the LLMs used are computationally intensive and require considerable computational resources, a significant limitation for accessibility. Furthermore, the sensitivity to prompt quality and potential for hallucination are inherent weaknesses of relying so heavily on LLMs.

**Strengths:**

*   **Problem Definition:** Clearly articulates the limitations of existing doctor recommendation systems.
*   **Methodology:** The LLM-based framework is well-designed and incorporates several important features, including pointwise ranking, query-specific ranking criteria, and explanation generation. The forced elicitation mechanism is a clever approach to ensure consistent LLM output.
*   **Dataset:** The DrRank dataset is a valuable contribution to the field.
*   **Experiments:** The paper presents extensive experimental results, comparing the proposed framework to strong baselines and analyzing fairness and interpretability.
*   **Fairness:** The fairness analysis is commendable.
*   **Reproducibility:** Open source code

**Weaknesses:**

*   **Computational Cost:** The computational intensity of LLMs limits the real-world practicality of the proposed framework.
*   **LLM Reliance:**  Over-reliance on the LLM with inherent weaknesses of hallucination, prompt dependence, and limited contextual understanding.
*   **Generalizability:** The DrRank dataset focuses primarily on a specific set of diseases and treatments. The generalizability of the framework to other medical domains needs further validation.
*   **Minor Justification Needed:** Some parts of the paper lack sufficient justification or evidence. For example, the empirical approach of tuning `sk=k` lacks justification on why the optimal values should be consecutive and start from `0`.
*   **Limited comparison with other methods in medical fields.** The paper lacks comparison with methods in the medical fields such as [7].
*   **Ranking criteria:** The ranking criteria is not clearly defined on what metrics are used for "comprehensiveness, practicality, and overall format".

**Justification for Score:**

The paper presents a solid contribution to the field of doctor recommendation. The novel LLM-based framework, coupled with the new DrRank dataset, addresses important limitations in existing systems, particularly around interpretability and expert-driven recommendation. The fairness analysis adds a critical layer of evaluation.

However, the LLM reliance and computational cost represent significant drawbacks. While the experimental results are impressive, the limited generalizability to other medical domains and the need for careful prompt engineering temper the excitement. Therefore, while this is a valuable step forward, some refinement is needed to have a broader practical impact.

Score: 7.5

- **Classification**: cs.IR
- **Score**: 7/10

### Diffusion-Based mmWave Radar Point Cloud Enhancement Driven by Range Images
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2503.02300v1)
- **Authors**: Ruixin Wu, Zihan Li, Jin Wang, Xiangyu Xu, Huan Yu, Zhi Zheng, Kaixiang Huang, Guodong Lu
- **Abstract**: Millimeter-wave (mmWave) radar has attracted significant attention in robotics and autonomous driving. However, despite the perception stability in harsh environments, the point cloud generated by mmWave radar is relatively sparse while containing significant noise, which limits its further development. Traditional mmWave radar enhancement approaches often struggle to leverage the effectiveness of diffusion models in super-resolution, largely due to the unnatural range-azimuth heatmap (RAH) or bird's eye view (BEV) representation. To overcome this limitation, we propose a novel method that pioneers the application of fusing range images with image diffusion models, achieving accurate and dense mmWave radar point clouds that are similar to LiDAR. Benefitting from the projection that aligns with human observation, the range image representation of mmWave radar is close to natural images, allowing the knowledge from pre-trained image diffusion models to be effectively transferred, significantly improving the overall performance. Extensive evaluations on both public datasets and self-constructed datasets demonstrate that our approach provides substantial improvements, establishing a new state-of-the-art performance in generating truly three-dimensional LiDAR-like point clouds via mmWave radar.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the paper:

**Summary:**

The paper introduces a novel method for enhancing mmWave radar point clouds by using diffusion models, specifically designed to generate high-quality, LiDAR-like point clouds.  The key innovation is to leverage range images (a representation more aligned with human perception) as an intermediary representation for the radar data.  This allows pre-trained image diffusion models to be effectively applied, as range images are more similar to natural images than the traditionally used range-azimuth heatmaps (RAH) or bird's-eye view (BEV) representations.  The method involves projecting both LiDAR (ground truth) and mmWave data into range images, training a diffusion model conditioned on the mmWave range images to generate LiDAR-like range images, and then back-projecting the generated range images to create the final enhanced point cloud.  The authors demonstrate significant improvements over existing methods on public and self-constructed datasets.

**Critical Evaluation:**

*   **Novelty:** The core idea of using range images as a bridge between mmWave radar data and image diffusion models for point cloud enhancement is relatively novel. While diffusion models have been applied to mmWave radar before, the use of range images and their benefits for leveraging pre-trained image models sets it apart. This addresses a key limitation of prior work, which struggled to effectively utilize pre-trained models due to the unnatural representations like RAH or BEV.

*   **Significance:** Improving the quality of mmWave radar point clouds is a significant problem in robotics and autonomous driving.  MmWave radar offers advantages over LiDAR in adverse weather conditions but suffers from sparsity and noise. The proposed method tackles these limitations and shows promising results in generating denser and more accurate point clouds. This can potentially broaden the usability of mmWave radar in various applications.
*   **Strengths:**
    *   **Clear Problem Definition:** The paper clearly articulates the problem with existing mmWave radar enhancement techniques and motivates the use of range images and diffusion models well.
    *   **Effective Approach:**  The approach is technically sound, with a well-defined pipeline for range image construction, diffusion model training, and point cloud generation.
    *   **Strong Experimental Results:** The experimental results demonstrate a clear improvement over baseline methods on multiple datasets, both qualitatively and quantitatively. The use of both public and self-constructed datasets adds to the credibility of the evaluation.
    *   **Well-Written and Organized:** The paper is generally well-written, logically organized, and easy to follow.

*   **Weaknesses:**
    *   **Computational Cost:** The paper doesn't explicitly address the computational cost of the method, which could be a limiting factor for real-time applications, given the use of diffusion models. Evaluating the inference time on an embedded system would strengthen the paper.
    *   **Reliance on LiDAR Data During Training:** The method relies on paired LiDAR data for training the diffusion model. This dependence may limit its applicability in scenarios where LiDAR data is not readily available. While this is often the case in research settings, it's an important consideration. The authors could explore techniques to reduce or eliminate this dependence in future work.
    *   **Limited Ablation Studies:** While the paper presents quantitative and qualitative comparisons, a more thorough ablation study analyzing the impact of different loss function components (MSE, LPIPS, distance loss) would strengthen the analysis.
    *   **Overclaiming:** Stating that the point clouds are "truly three-dimensional LiDAR-like point clouds" is a bit of an overclaim.  While the results are promising, there will still be differences in characteristics, data structure, and information captured compared to native LiDAR point clouds.

*   **Potential Influence:**  The paper has the potential to influence the field of mmWave radar perception by introducing a more effective way to integrate radar data with deep generative models.  It opens up new avenues for research in radar enhancement, and could lead to improved performance in robotics and autonomous driving applications.
*   **Justification of Score:** The score reflects the novel approach, significant results, and potential influence. However, the limitations regarding computational cost, LiDAR data dependence, and some potential overclaiming slightly temper the overall assessment. The approach could potentially enhance the adoption of mmWave radar into broader robotics and autonomous driving applications where LiDAR fails to deliver accurate perceptions.

**Score: 7.5**

- **Classification**: cs.RO
- **Score**: 7/10

### A Token-level Text Image Foundation Model for Document Understanding
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2503.02304v1)
- **Authors**: Tongkun Guan, Zining Wang, Pei Fu, Zhengtao Guo, Wei Shen, Kai Zhou, Tiezhu Yue, Chen Duan, Hao Sun, Qianyi Jiang, Junfeng Luo, Xiaokang Yang
- **Abstract**: In recent years, general visual foundation models (VFMs) have witnessed increasing adoption, particularly as image encoders for popular multi-modal large language models (MLLMs). However, without semantically fine-grained supervision, these models still encounter fundamental prediction errors in the context of downstream text-image-related tasks, i.e., perception, understanding and reasoning with images containing small and dense texts. To bridge this gap, we develop TokenOCR, the first token-level visual foundation model specifically tailored for text-image-related tasks, designed to support a variety of traditional downstream applications. To facilitate the pretraining of TokenOCR, we also devise a high-quality data production pipeline that constructs the first token-level image text dataset, TokenIT, comprising 20 million images and 1.8 billion token-mask pairs. Furthermore, leveraging this foundation with exceptional image-as-text capability, we seamlessly replace previous VFMs with TokenOCR to construct a document-level MLLM, TokenVL, for VQA-based document understanding tasks. Finally, extensive experiments demonstrate the effectiveness of TokenOCR and TokenVL. Code, datasets, and weights will be available at https://token-family.github.io/TokenOCR_project.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the paper:

**Summary:**

The paper introduces TokenOCR, a novel token-level text image foundation model specifically designed for document understanding tasks.  It addresses the limitations of existing visual foundation models (VFMs) in handling fine-grained text perception, understanding, and reasoning in images containing small and dense text. The authors construct a new dataset, TokenIT, consisting of 20 million images and 1.8 billion token-mask pairs.  TokenOCR is trained on TokenIT, aligning token-level visual embeddings with corresponding language embeddings.  The model's ability to act as a drop-in replacement for previous VFMs is demonstrated by constructing TokenVL, a document-level MLLM for VQA-based document understanding.  Extensive experiments showcase the effectiveness of TokenOCR and TokenVL on a range of downstream tasks, including text segmentation, retrieval, and VQA. The paper claims to demonstrate superior performance compared to existing VFMs like CLIP, SAM, and InternViT2.5.

**Critical Evaluation:**

*   **Novelty:** The core novelty lies in the **token-level approach to VFM pre-training**.  Existing VFMs typically operate at the image-level or pixel-level. While other works incorporate high resolution features (like SAM), the idea of constructing a VFM specifically around token-level alignment is a distinct contribution. The self-constructed TokenIT dataset is also a valuable addition, although the reliance on public data means that the core data itself isn't necessarily fundamentally novel. The construction of TokenVL as a demonstration of TokenOCR's utility reinforces the application focus.

*   **Significance:** The significance stems from addressing a **practical limitation** of current VFMs. Documents often contain densely packed text and small text sizes, making it crucial to have models capable of fine-grained perception. Successfully demonstrating that TokenOCR improves performance in document understanding tasks increases its significance. The potential to influence how MLLMs handle document-related tasks gives merit to its application.

*   **Strengths:**

    *   **Clear Problem Definition:** The paper clearly identifies a weakness of current VFMs in handling dense and small text within images.

    *   **Novel Approach:**  The token-level VFM approach is a distinct and potentially impactful idea.

    *   **Dataset Contribution:**  TokenIT contributes a substantial new dataset to the field.

    *   **Extensive Experiments:** The paper presents a thorough evaluation across various downstream tasks, demonstrating the practical benefits of TokenOCR.

    *   **Open-Source and Reproducible:** The promise of releasing code, datasets, and weights increases the impact by enabling further research and adoption.

*   **Weaknesses:**

    *   **TokenIT Data Generation Pipeline Details:** While the scale of TokenIT is impressive, the details of the data generation pipeline should be clear and reproducible, specifically the use of unsupervised clustering.
