### An Efficient and Precise Training Data Construction Framework for Process-supervised Reward Model in Mathematical Reasoning
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2503.02382v1)
- **Authors**: Wei Sun, Qianlong Du, Fuwei Cui, Jiajun Zhang
- **Abstract**: Enhancing the mathematical reasoning capabilities of Large Language Models (LLMs) is of great scientific and practical significance. Researchers typically employ process-supervised reward models (PRMs) to guide the reasoning process, effectively improving the models' reasoning abilities. However, existing methods for constructing process supervision training data, such as manual annotation and per-step Monte Carlo estimation, are often costly or suffer from poor quality. To address these challenges, this paper introduces a framework called EpicPRM, which annotates each intermediate reasoning step based on its quantified contribution and uses an adaptive binary search algorithm to enhance both annotation precision and efficiency. Using this approach, we efficiently construct a high-quality process supervision training dataset named Epic50k, consisting of 50k annotated intermediate steps. Compared to other publicly available datasets, the PRM trained on Epic50k demonstrates significantly superior performance. Getting Epic50k at https://github.com/xiaolizh1/EpicPRM.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the provided research paper:

**Summary:**

The paper introduces EpicPRM, a framework for efficient and precise construction of process-supervised reward models (PRMs) for mathematical reasoning. The framework addresses the limitations of existing methods, namely manual annotation (expensive, difficult to scale) and automatic annotation (lower quality). EpicPRM annotates intermediate reasoning steps based on their quantified contribution to the solution using perplexity (PPL) rather than simple counts, and employs an adaptive binary search algorithm to efficiently identify the first erroneous step. This reduces annotation costs significantly.  They create a dataset, Epic50k, and show that PRMs trained on it outperform those trained on larger publicly available datasets like PRM800k and Math-Shepherd, demonstrating the importance of data quality over quantity.  The core of the paper lies in quantifying the contribution of each step to the final answer and using this information to guide the annotation process.

**Critical Evaluation:**

*   **Novelty:** The novelty of the paper lies in its integrated approach to building high-quality PRMs.  While the individual components -- binary search, Monte Carlo estimation, perplexity measure, and the focus on identifying *only* the first error -- are not entirely new, their combination and application in this specific context is innovative.  The key innovation appears to be the contribution-based annotation method and the adaptive binary search tailored to problem difficulty.  The use of perplexity instead of simply counting successful rollouts is a good improvement to Monte Carlo Estimation and demonstrates a deeper understanding of LLM probabilities.

*   **Significance:** The paper addresses a critical bottleneck in training effective PRMs: the cost and quality of annotation. By significantly reducing annotation costs while improving data quality, EpicPRM has the potential to democratize the development and use of PRMs, allowing researchers with limited computational resources to train competitive models. The findings demonstrate the importance of *quality* in training data, challenging the common assumption that simply scaling up datasets will always yield better results. The improved performance over PRM800k using a much smaller dataset is a strong argument for the method's efficacy.

*   **Strengths:**

    *   **Strong Empirical Results:** The experiments clearly demonstrate the superiority of EpicPRM-trained models compared to models trained on existing datasets. The experiments are well-designed with multiple generators being tested across varying temperatures. The ablation studies, particularly the comparison against randomly selected subsets of larger datasets, provide compelling evidence for the method's effectiveness.
    *   **Practical Impact:** The paper provides a practical solution to a real-world problem, and makes the implementation available. The reduction of annotation costs and the potential for broader adoption of PRMs are valuable contributions to the field.
    *   **Well-Written and Clear:** The paper is well-written and easy to understand. The method is clearly explained, and the experimental results are presented in a convincing manner.
    *   **Adaptive sampling strategy:** Addressing the problem with different difficulties and adjusting the generation budget, the results is promising.

*   **Weaknesses:**

    *   **Limited Scope:** The experiments are primarily focused on mathematical reasoning. While this is an important domain, it remains to be seen how well EpicPRM generalizes to other reasoning tasks, such as commonsense reasoning or logical inference. Although Section 4.6 touches on out of domain generalization, a more comprehensive study on a wider variety of benchmark tasks would strengthen the paper.
    *   **Gold Standard Dependency:** The reliance on gold standard answers is a potential limitation. While unavoidable in many settings, it restricts the method's applicability to tasks where such answers are available. Also, how this gold standard affects the results, is also not discussed.
    *   **LLM dependency:** The method relies on LLMs, which have their own biases and inaccuracies. Although using multiple LLMs as completers and calculating the average can help alleviate these issues, there is still a strong dependence on the quality of the LLMs used. How sensitive are the results to different LLM versions and LLM fine tuning?
    *   **Average length of the solution:** The average solution in the Epic50k is 10 steps. It is unclear how the initial search position changes with higher steps.

*   **Potential Influence:** The paper is likely to have a significant influence on the field. Other researchers are now more equipped to create supervised process model in limited compute. The focus on quality over quantity will likely inspire further research into data curation and annotation strategies. The open-source nature of the framework will facilitate its adoption and extension by the broader community.

**Justification for Score:**

EpicPRM represents a significant advancement in the field of process-supervised reward models. The innovative combination of existing techniques, the strong empirical results, and the potential for broad impact justify a high score. While the limitations mentioned above exist, they do not detract significantly from the paper's overall contribution. The importance is further highlighted by the comparison against other methods in the market that focus on the overall answer rather than the process supervision. The paper's demonstration that high-quality supervision can drastically lower the cost and effort to build a PRM represents a meaningful step towards enabling wider utilization of these powerful models for reasoning.

Score: 8

- **Classification**: cs.CL
- **Score**: 8/10

### ReSo: A Reward-driven Self-organizing LLM-based Multi-Agent System for Reasoning Tasks
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2503.02390v1)
- **Authors**: Heng Zhou, Hejia Geng, Xiangyuan Xue, Zhenfei Yin, Lei Bai
- **Abstract**: Multi-agent systems have emerged as a promising approach for enhancing the reasoning capabilities of large language models in complex problem-solving. However, current MAS frameworks are limited by poor flexibility and scalability, with underdeveloped optimization strategies. To address these challenges, we propose ReSo, which integrates task graph generation with a reward-driven two-stage agent selection process. The core of ReSo is the proposed Collaborative Reward Model, which can provide fine-grained reward signals for MAS cooperation for optimization. We also introduce an automated data synthesis framework for generating MAS benchmarks, without human annotations. Experimentally, ReSo matches or outperforms existing methods. ReSo achieves \textbf{33.7\%} and \textbf{32.3\%} accuracy on Math-MAS and SciBench-MAS SciBench, while other methods completely fail. Code is available at: \href{https://github.com/hengzzzhou/ReSo}{ReSo}
- **Summary**: Okay, here's a summary and a critical evaluation of the paper:

**Summary**

The paper introduces ReSo, a reward-driven self-organizing multi-agent system (MAS) designed to improve the reasoning capabilities of large language models (LLMs) in complex problem-solving. The approach addresses limitations of existing MAS frameworks, such as poor flexibility, scalability, and underdeveloped optimization strategies.  ReSo integrates task graph generation with a two-stage agent selection process guided by a novel Collaborative Reward Model (CRM). CRM provides fine-grained reward signals to optimize MAS cooperation. Additionally, the paper presents an automated data synthesis framework to generate MAS benchmarks without human annotation. Experimental results show that ReSo matches or outperforms existing methods, particularly on challenging datasets like Math-MAS and SciBench-MAS, where other methods fail. The code and data are released.

**Critical Evaluation**

* **Strengths:**

    *   **Addresses a real problem:** The paper tackles the important challenge of improving LLM reasoning through MAS. The identified limitations of current MAS frameworks, especially in adaptability and scalability, are valid and well-articulated.
    *   **Novelty in the approach:** The combination of task graph generation, a two-stage agent selection process, and the Collaborative Reward Model (CRM) represents a meaningful step forward. The CRM, in particular, is a potentially valuable contribution as it seeks to provide more granular feedback for agent collaboration compared to existing methods.
    *   **Data synthesis:** The automated data synthesis framework is a practical and useful contribution. The scarcity of high-quality MAS evaluation benchmarks is a known issue, and the proposed framework offers a way to generate arbitrarily complex MAS tasks from existing LLM datasets, easing the evaluation process, although this does introduce biases, described in the weaknesses.
    *   **Strong empirical results:** The experimental results, particularly the success on Math-MAS and SciBench-MAS where other methods fail, are compelling and support the effectiveness of ReSo.
    *   **Open sourcing:** Releasing the code and data facilitates reproducibility and further research.

* **Weaknesses:**

    *   **Dependence on underlying LLMs:** The performance of ReSo heavily relies on the capabilities of the underlying LLMs used for task decomposition, agent selection, and individual agent reasoning. While the paper acknowledges this, a more in-depth discussion of how the choice of LLMs affects the overall performance and scalability would be beneficial.
    *   **Data Synthesis Framework Limits:** While beneficial, the automated dataset generation method introduces biases from the source LLM datasets, limiting the diversity and complexity of the scenarios it generates. This limits the generalizability and broader applicability of the data, which could negatively impact the reliability and comprehensiveness of the results.
    *   **Agent Selection Complexity:** While two-stage agent selection is meant to be efficient, the use of UCB and the CRM both add computational overhead and can be hard to tune appropriately, impacting scalability in real-world applications.
    *   **Limited Ablation Studies:** While some ablation studies are present, a deeper dive into the individual contributions of each component (task graph generation, the agent selection and the CRM) would strengthen the paper, especially in a controlled setting.
    *   **Lack of Qualitative Analysis:** The paper primarily focuses on quantitative results. Including qualitative examples of ReSo's reasoning process, agent collaboration, and error analysis would provide more insights into the system's strengths and weaknesses.
    *   **Assumptions about Agent Abilities:** While the paper claims to address that existing MAS frameworks assume agent abilities are known, the DADB still requires an initial assessment.

*   **Significance:** The work is significant as it provides a useful framework for improving LLM reasoning and solving complex problems by allowing them to utilize MAS's more efficiently and flexibly. ReSo's potential to automate the design and optimization of MAS is a valuable contribution.

*   **Novelty:** The *combination* of self-organization, collaborative reward models, and automated data synthesis is novel. While individual components have been explored, the integrated approach is a significant contribution.

**Justification for Score:**

The paper presents a good advance in LLM-based reasoning by addressing key shortcomings in current MAS frameworks. The results are promising, and the techniques are novel and well-integrated. However, limitations exist around data synthesis biasing, complexity of implementation, and further need for qualitative analysis to validate the system.

Therefore, while impactful and with high future potential, some areas for improvement and investigation are needed.

**Score: 7.5**

- **Classification**: cs.MA
- **Score**: 7/10

### BHViT: Binarized Hybrid Vision Transformer
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2503.02394v1)
- **Authors**: Tian Gao, Yu Zhang, Zhiyuan Zhang, Huajun Liu, Kaijie Yin, Chengzhong Xu, Hui Kong
- **Abstract**: Model binarization has made significant progress in enabling real-time and energy-efficient computation for convolutional neural networks (CNN), offering a potential solution to the deployment challenges faced by Vision Transformers (ViTs) on edge devices. However, due to the structural differences between CNN and Transformer architectures, simply applying binary CNN strategies to the ViT models will lead to a significant performance drop. To tackle this challenge, we propose BHViT, a binarization-friendly hybrid ViT architecture and its full binarization model with the guidance of three important observations. Initially, BHViT utilizes the local information interaction and hierarchical feature aggregation technique from coarse to fine levels to address redundant computations stemming from excessive tokens. Then, a novel module based on shift operations is proposed to enhance the performance of the binary Multilayer Perceptron (MLP) module without significantly increasing computational overhead. In addition, an innovative attention matrix binarization method based on quantization decomposition is proposed to evaluate the token's importance in the binarized attention matrix. Finally, we propose a regularization loss to address the inadequate optimization caused by the incompatibility between the weight oscillation in the binary layers and the Adam Optimizer. Extensive experimental results demonstrate that our proposed algorithm achieves SOTA performance among binary ViT methods.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the paper "BHVIT: Binarized Hybrid Vision Transformer":

**Summary:**

The paper proposes a binarized hybrid Vision Transformer (BHViT) architecture designed for efficient deployment on edge devices.  The key contributions focus on addressing performance degradation issues encountered when directly applying binary CNN strategies to ViTs. The authors propose three novel modules: 1) a hybrid architecture utilizing local information interaction and hierarchical feature aggregation to reduce redundant computations, 2) a shift operation-based module to enhance the performance of the binary MLP, and 3) a quantization decomposition method for attention matrix binarization. They also introduce a regularization loss to address optimization issues arising from the incompatibility between binary layer weight oscillations and the Adam optimizer. Experimental results demonstrate state-of-the-art performance compared to other binary ViT methods on image classification and segmentation tasks.

**Critical Evaluation:**

**Novelty:** The paper exhibits decent novelty through its three proposed modules, especially the specific design considerations for binarizing ViTs. The idea of using a hybrid CNN-Transformer approach tailored for binarization is sound. Quantization decomposition of attention matrices is a noteworthy approach to mitigate the information loss during binarization. The regularization loss is a sensible response to observed training instability. However, the individual components are somewhat incremental advances building upon existing binarization techniques, although adapted well to the ViT architecture. The shift-based MLP enhancement module is less conceptually groundbreaking, but contributes practically to the overall performance.

**Significance:** The significance lies in enabling efficient deployment of ViTs on resource-constrained devices. Binarization is a crucial technique to reduce model size and computational cost, making complex architectures like ViTs more practical for edge applications. Achieving state-of-the-art performance among binary ViTs contributes to the wider adoption of Transformers in real-world scenarios. Also, identifying specific optimization difficulties encountered when training binary ViTs offers valuable insights for future research.

**Strengths:**
*   The paper clearly identifies a problem (performance degradation of binary ViTs) and motivates its approach well.
*   The proposed modules are technically sound and well-explained.
*   The experimental results are convincing, demonstrating superior performance on several datasets.
*   The ablation study effectively validates the contribution of each proposed module.
*   The source code is released, which fosters reproducibility and allows the research community to build upon their work.

**Weaknesses:**
*   While the modules are novel in their combination and application to ViTs, the individual components are not exceptionally innovative compared to general binarization methods.
*   The description of some module designs could be more detailed and insightful. For instance, while shift operations for MLP enhancement is mentioned, a deeper analysis of why/how it improves performance would be beneficial.
*   There is little discussion about the limitations of the approach.  For example, how well does BHViT generalize to different ViT architectures or datasets?
*   The efficiency analysis (latency) only considers ARM Cortex-A76 which is a limited evaluation setting. Broader range of devices would significantly increase the impact.

**Potential Influence:**
The paper is likely to influence future research on efficient ViTs. It highlights key challenges in binarizing ViTs and proposes practical solutions. The open-sourced code can serve as a valuable resource for researchers and practitioners in the field. Given the growing interest in deploying deep learning models on edge devices, this research can facilitate the development of more efficient and practical ViT-based solutions.

**Justification for Score:**
The paper contributes meaningfully to the field of efficient deep learning and, specifically, binary Vision Transformers. The design is novel and builds upon existing methods. A reasonable argument for the score would be that this is a good engineering advancement, but it is not a theoretical breakthrough or introducing entirely new concepts. It falls somewhere in the range of having important practical impacts, solid contributions, and a few noticeable weaknesses.

**Score: 7.5**

- **Classification**: cs.CV
- **Score**: 7/10

### PersonaX: A Recommendation Agent Oriented User Modeling Framework for Long Behavior Sequence
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2503.02398v1)
- **Authors**: Yunxiao Shi, Wujiang Xu, Zeqi Zhang, Xing Zi, Qiang Wu, Min Xu
- **Abstract**: Recommendation agents leverage large language models for user modeling LLM UM to construct textual personas guiding alignment with real users. However existing LLM UM methods struggle with long user generated content UGC due to context limitations and performance degradation. To address this sampling strategies prioritize relevance or recency are often applied yet they inevitably neglect the diverse user interests embedded within the discarded behaviors resulting in incomplete modeling and degraded profiling quality. Furthermore relevance based sampling requires real time retrieval forcing the user modeling process to operate online which introduces significant latency overhead. In this paper we propose PersonaX an agent agnostic LLM UM framework that tackles these challenges through sub behavior sequence SBS selection and offline multi persona construction. PersonaX extracts compact SBS segments offline to capture diverse user interests generating fine grained textual personas that are cached for efficient online retrieval. This approach ensures that the user persona used for prompting remains highly relevant to the current context while eliminating the need for online user modeling. For SBS selection we ensure both efficiency length less than five and high representational quality by balancing prototypicality and diversity within the sampled data. Extensive experiments validate the effectiveness and versatility of PersonaX in high quality user profiling. Utilizing only 30 to 50 percent of the behavioral data with a sequence length of 480 integrating PersonaX with AgentCF yields an absolute performance improvement of 3 to 11 percent while integration with Agent4Rec results in a gain of 10 to 50 percent. PersonaX as an agent agnostic framework sets a new benchmark for scalable user modeling paving the way for more accurate and efficient LLM driven recommendation agents.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the paper "PersonaX: A Recommendation Agent-Oriented User Modeling Framework for Long Behavior Sequence":

**Summary:**

The paper introduces PersonaX, a new LLM-UM framework for user modeling specifically designed to handle long user behavior sequences in recommendation agents.  It addresses the challenges of context limitations and performance degradation that existing methods face with extensive user-generated content (UGC). PersonaX operates offline, extracting representative short sub-sequences (SBS) from the user's history to capture diverse interests.  These SBS segments are used to construct fine-grained, multi-dimensional user personas, which are then cached for efficient online retrieval during recommendation.  The framework's key features include: (1) a novel SBS selection strategy that balances prototypicality and diversity, (2) an agent-agnostic design for integration with existing LLM-UM methods, and (3) offline persona construction for reduced online latency.  Experimental results on several datasets show improved ranking accuracy and online inference efficiency compared to baselines.

**Critical Evaluation:**

*   **Novelty:** The paper's novelty lies in its specific combination of techniques for handling long behavioral sequences within an LLM-UM context, with specific contributions in the offline extraction of representative sub-sequences and the balancing of prototypicality and diversity in the SBS selection. While individual components like behavior summarization and reflection exist, the integration and offline approach aimed at improving both accuracy and efficiency in long sequence scenarios is a distinct contribution.

*   **Significance:** The paper addresses a significant challenge in applying LLM-UM to real-world recommendation systems: effectively processing long user histories. The proposed solution of offline processing and balanced SBS selection offers a practical approach to mitigate the input length limitations and performance degradation associated with LLMs. The performance gains demonstrated across different datasets and LLM-UM methods underscore the potential of PersonaX to improve recommendation accuracy and efficiency. The agent-agnostic nature also adds to its significance, as it can be readily integrated into diverse existing agent architectures.

*   **Strengths:**

    *   **Clear problem definition:** The paper clearly identifies the limitations of existing LLM-UM methods when dealing with long behavior sequences.
    *   **Well-defined framework:** PersonaX is presented as a coherent and modular framework with a clearly defined workflow.
    *   **Effective SBS selection strategy:** The approach to balancing prototypicality and diversity appears to be a key strength, addressing the shortcomings of purely relevance- or recency-based sampling.
    *   **Comprehensive experiments:** The experiments cover multiple datasets, metrics, and comparisons to several strong baselines.
    *   **Agent-agnostic approach:** PersonaX is agent-agnostic which improves its versatality

*   **Weaknesses:**

    *   **Dependency on Clustering Quality:** Performance relies on the hierarchical clustering method, while well-explained, the potential limitation regarding the complexity of large datasets might be present.
    *   **Limited Analysis of Hyperparameter Sensitivity:** While the paper discusses hyperparameter tuning, a more thorough analysis of their sensitivity could strengthen the practical guidance for practitioners. It would be particularly useful to understand how the optimal parameters vary across different types of datasets or user behaviors.
    *   **Computational Overhead of Offline Profiling:** The paper focuses on online efficiency gains but lacks a detailed analysis of the computational cost associated with the offline persona construction.
    *   **Lack of real-world deployment:** The experiments are conducted in a controlled setting, and the paper does not provide insights into the challenges and trade-offs associated with deploying PersonaX in a live, production environment.
    *   **Reliance on OpenAI's LLMs:** The experiments rely on a closed-source LLM model, which may limit the reproducibility of the results.

*   **Potential Influence:** The paper provides a valuable approach for scaling LLM-UM to handle long user sequences, potentially influencing the design of future recommendation systems. The balanced SBS selection strategy could be adopted or adapted in other UM tasks. The offline persona construction approach has clear implications for improving online efficiency.

*   **Rigorous Rationale for Score:** The paper presents a sound methodology and demonstrates promising results, addressing a clearly defined problem. The novelty is in the specific integration and adaptation of existing techniques, resulting in a practical and effective framework. However, the reliance on a specific LLM, limited sensitivity analysis, and lack of real-world deployment insights prevent it from achieving a higher score. The novelty is also somewhat incremental, building on existing summarization and reflection-based techniques.

**Score: 7**

- **Classification**: cs.IR
- **Score**: 7/10

### Promptware Engineering: Software Engineering for LLM Prompt Development
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2503.02400v1)
- **Authors**: Zhenpeng Chen, Chong Wang, Weisong Sun, Guang Yang, Xuanzhe Liu, Jie M. Zhang, Yang Liu
- **Abstract**: Large Language Models (LLMs) are increasingly integrated into software applications, with prompts serving as the primary 'programming' interface to guide their behavior. As a result, a new software paradigm, promptware, has emerged, using natural language prompts to interact with LLMs and enabling complex tasks without traditional coding. Unlike traditional software, which relies on formal programming languages and deterministic runtime environments, promptware is based on ambiguous, unstructured, and context-dependent natural language and operates on LLMs as runtime environments, which are probabilistic and non-deterministic. These fundamental differences introduce unique challenges in prompt development. In practice, prompt development is largely ad hoc and experimental, relying on a time-consuming trial-and-error process - a challenge we term the 'promptware crisis.' To address this, we propose promptware engineering, a new methodology that adapts established software engineering principles to the process of prompt development. Building on decades of success in traditional software engineering, we envision a systematic framework that includes prompt requirements engineering, design, implementation, testing, debugging, and evolution. Unlike traditional software engineering, our framework is specifically tailored to the unique characteristics of prompt development. This paper outlines a comprehensive roadmap for promptware engineering, identifying key research directions and offering actionable insights to advance LLM-based software development.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the provided paper:

**Summary:**

The paper "Promptware Engineering: Software Engineering for LLM Prompt Development" argues that the development of prompts for Large Language Models (LLMs) is currently ad-hoc and lacks the systematic rigor of traditional software engineering. The authors coin the term "promptware" to describe LLM-based software where prompts serve as the primary interface. They propose "promptware engineering" as a new methodology that applies established software engineering principles to prompt development, aiming to address the "promptware crisis" – the challenges of managing complex and unreliable prompt-based systems. The paper outlines a comprehensive roadmap, identifying key research directions within prompt requirements engineering, design, implementation, testing, debugging, and evolution. It contrasts traditional software paradigms with the unique characteristics of promptware relating to language (natural vs. formal) and runtime environments (deterministic vs. probabilistic LLMs), and offers 24 specific research opportunities to advance LLM-based software development.

**Critical Evaluation:**

*   **Novelty:** The concept of "promptware engineering" and framing prompt development as a software engineering problem is relatively novel. While the connection between SE and prompt engineering has been explored, this paper provides a more comprehensive framework, highlighting specific areas for cross-pollination of ideas. The comparison of promptware with traditional software, based on language and runtime environment characteristics, is a valuable contribution. However, the individual elements proposed within the roadmap (e.g., prompt libraries, formal design patterns, testing) are not entirely new, but rather re-interpretations of existing SE practices in the context of LLMs.

*   **Significance:** The paper addresses a critical gap in the LLM application development lifecycle. As LLMs become increasingly integrated into software, the need for systematic prompt management and reliable performance becomes paramount. The paper's roadmap provides a valuable starting point for researchers and practitioners looking to improve the development process. By applying SE principles, the authors aim to increase prompt reliability, reduce development time, and improve overall software quality.

*   **Strengths:**

    *   **Clear articulation of the problem:** The paper effectively highlights the challenges of ad-hoc prompt engineering and motivates the need for a more structured approach. The "promptware crisis" terminology is memorable and aptly captures the problem.
    *   **Comprehensive roadmap:** The identified research opportunities cover a wide range of topics and provide concrete directions for future work.
    *   **Detailed comparison:**  The detailed comparison between traditional software and promptware based on their languages and runtime environments provides solid reasoning for the presented vision.
    *   **Actionable insights:** The paper translates abstract SE principles into concrete suggestions applicable to prompt engineering.
    *   **Good overall organization:** The paper is well structured and easy to follow.

*   **Weaknesses:**

    *   **Limited practical validation:** The paper primarily presents a conceptual framework without significant empirical evaluation or implementation of the proposed techniques. While illustrative examples are given to the outlined research directions, demonstrating their efficacy would significantly strengthen the argument.
    *   **Overly broad scope:** While comprehensiveness is a strength, the paper attempts to cover too many aspects, potentially lacking depth in specific areas. Some of the research opportunities are high-level and could benefit from further elaboration.
    *   **Reliance on analogy:** The paper relies heavily on analogies to traditional software engineering. While helpful for understanding, a more nuanced discussion of the specific adaptations and limitations of SE principles in the context of promptware would be beneficial. LLMs have fundamentally different limitations than traditional software, and simple analogy is not always sufficient.
    *   **LLM-as-runtime is a slightly misleading construct:** LLMs aren't a runtime in the traditional sense. It has more in common with a black-box service that handles requests, the equivalent of an API call, rather than a complete running process.
    *   **Overstates challenges:** The paper overstates the challenges of deterministic testing as this depends heavily on the LLM used and its temperature setting. More deterministic LLMs exist, as well as low temperature settings.
    *   **Prompt engineering, generally, has a lower bar than programming in most cases.** The ease of iteration tends to hide or solve many of the challenges that software engineering principles solve. These issues appear in larger applications.

*   **Potential Impact:** The paper has the potential to influence the field by:

    *   Establishing promptware engineering as a legitimate area of research.
    *   Providing a common vocabulary and framework for discussing prompt development challenges.
    *   Inspiring new tools and techniques for managing and optimizing prompts.
    *   Encouraging interdisciplinary collaboration between computer scientists, linguists, and psychologists.

**Score:** 6/10

**Justification:**

The paper presents a valuable perspective on the need for more systematic approaches to prompt development. The "promptware engineering" framework is a useful contribution. The roadmap clearly outlines key research directions. The significant weakness is the lack of empirical validation. Most, if not all, of the ideas still require practical testing to see if the translated SE principles remain useful and applicable in this new domain. This, and some weaknesses in arguments based on general statements about "all LLMs" rather than more nuanced discussions that take into account variability in LLM design bring the score down to 6.

- **Classification**: cs.SE
- **Score**: 6/10

### Through the Static: Demystifying Malware Visualization via Explainability
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2503.02441v1)
- **Authors**: Matteo Brosolo, Vinod Puthuvath, Mauro Conti
- **Abstract**: Security researchers grapple with the surge of malicious files, necessitating swift identification and classification of malware strains for effective protection. Visual classifiers and in particular Convolutional Neural Networks (CNNs) have emerged as vital tools for this task. However, issues of robustness and explainability, common in other high risk domain like medicine and autonomous vehicles, remain understudied in current literature. Although deep learning visualization classifiers presented in research obtain great results without the need for expert feature extraction, they have not been properly studied in terms of their replicability. Additionally, the literature is not clear on how these types of classifiers arrive to their answers. Our study addresses these gaps by replicating six CNN models and exploring their pitfalls. We employ Class Activation Maps (CAMs), like GradCAM and HiResCAM, to assess model explainability. We evaluate the CNNs' performance and interpretability on two standard datasets, MalImg and Big2015, and a newly created called VX-Zoo. We employ these different CAM techniques to gauge the explainability of each of the models. With these tools, we investigate the underlying factors contributing to different interpretations of inputs across the different models, empowering human researchers to discern patterns crucial for identifying distinct malware families and explain why CNN models arrive at their conclusions. Other then highlighting the patterns found in the interpretability study, we employ the extracted heatmpas to enhance Visual Transformers classifiers' performance and explanation quality. This approach yields substantial improvements in F1 score, ranging from 2% to 8%, across the datasets compared to benchmark values.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the paper "Through the Static: Demystifying Malware Visualization via Explainability":

**Summary:**

The paper addresses the challenges of replicability and explainability in malware classification using visual representation and Convolutional Neural Networks (CNNs). The authors replicate six CNN models from existing literature, highlighting the difficulties in achieving identical results due to missing implementation details. They then explore the use of Class Activation Maps (CAMs), particularly HiResCAM, to understand the CNNs' decision-making processes.  They create a new malware dataset (VX-Zoo) to broaden their evaluation, and introduce a novel image masking technique using heatmaps to improve classifier performance. Finally, the paper integrates these approaches into a Visual Transformer (ViT) classifier to demonstrate performance gains.

**Critical Evaluation:**

*   **Strengths:**
    *   **Addresses Important Gaps:** The paper tackles the understudied aspects of replicability and explainability in the context of malware visualization, which are vital for trust and security.
    *   **Comprehensive Replication Study:**  The attempt to replicate several CNN models is valuable, documenting the practical challenges often glossed over in publications.
    *   **Novel Masking Technique:** The image masking approach using CAMs is a significant contribution, providing a way to leverage explainability to enhance classifier performance. This demonstrates a clear link between understanding *why* a model makes a decision and improving its accuracy.
    *   **HiResCAM Focus:** The specific use of HiResCAM over GradCAM is well-justified, acknowledging its superior ability to provide detailed and faithful explanations.
    *   **New Dataset:** Creation of the VX-Zoo dataset contributes to the resources available for research in this area, and its unique characteristics (new samples, year-wise identification) address some limitations of existing datasets.
    *   **ViT Integration:** The use of Visual Transformers for classification with modified malware images is a relatively unexplored approach, so incorporating a VIT-based classifier is commendable.
    *   **Clear Presentation:** The paper is well-written and structured, with clearly defined research questions, methodologies, and results.

*   **Weaknesses:**
    *   **Incremental Contribution:** While the masking technique is novel, the underlying approach of using CNNs for malware image classification is well-established. The novelty primarily resides in the specific combination of techniques and the new dataset.
    *   **Limited ViT Exploration:** The ViT implementation, described as "minimalist" in terms of implementation and analysis, doesn't explore its full potential and seems more like a proof-of-concept.
    *   **Subjectivity in Heatmap Interpretation:** While the paper acknowledges the subjectivity of heatmap interpretation, it doesn't fully address the challenge of translating these insights into actionable malware analysis knowledge.
    *   **Dataset limitations** Despite the novelty, the dataset has 15 malware families, which might not represent the overall diversity in malware samples across all categories.

*   **Novelty and Significance:**
    *   The masking technique and its use in improving ViT performance offers a practical approach to using explainability in malware classification.
    *   The replication study provides valuable insights into the practical challenges of reproducing published results.

*   **Impact on the Field:** The paper has the potential to influence future research by:
    *   Highlighting the importance of replicability and complete code sharing.
    *   Promoting the use of explainability techniques for understanding and improving malware classifiers.
    *   Providing a new dataset for benchmarking malware classification algorithms.

**Rationale:**

The paper makes solid contributions to the field, particularly in bridging the gap between explainability and performance enhancement in malware classification. The replication study underscores practical challenges, and the masking technique is a novel and effective approach. However, the overall contribution, while valuable, is incremental building upon existing concepts. The ViT exploration is somewhat limited, reducing the overall impact.

### AILS-NTUA at SemEval-2025 Task 3: Leveraging Large Language Models and Translation Strategies for Multilingual Hallucination Detection
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2503.02442v1)
- **Authors**: Dimitra Karkani, Maria Lymperaiou, Giorgos Filandrianos, Nikolaos Spanos, Athanasios Voulodimos, Giorgos Stamou
- **Abstract**: Multilingual hallucination detection stands as an underexplored challenge, which the Mu-SHROOM shared task seeks to address. In this work, we propose an efficient, training-free LLM prompting strategy that enhances detection by translating multilingual text spans into English. Our approach achieves competitive rankings across multiple languages, securing two first positions in low-resource languages. The consistency of our results highlights the effectiveness of our translation strategy for hallucination detection, demonstrating its applicability regardless of the source language.
- **Summary**: Here's a concise summary and critical evaluation of the AILS-NTUA at SemEval-2025 Task 3 paper:

**Summary:**

The paper presents a system for multilingual hallucination detection, participating in the Mu-SHROOM shared task at SemEval-2025. Their approach leverages Large Language Models (LLMs) – specifically, Llama 3 and Claude – prompted in a few-shot manner. A key strategy involves translating multilingual text spans into English to enhance detection accuracy. The system achieves competitive results across multiple languages, including first-place positions in Farsi and Czech (low-resource languages) and a second-place position in Italian. The paper explores various prompting and translation strategies, concluding that translating input and output pairs into English using an external translation system is highly effective.

**Critical Evaluation:**

*   **Novelty:** The novelty lies primarily in the practical application of LLM prompting and translation strategies to a challenging multilingual task. The idea of using LLMs for hallucination detection isn't entirely new, nor is the use of translation for cross-lingual tasks. However, the specific combination of few-shot prompting with English translation, along with a detailed ablation study of different prompting and translation techniques, constitutes a valuable contribution. The emphasis on low-resource languages is a plus.

*   **Significance:** Multilingual hallucination detection is a crucial problem. LLMs are increasingly deployed in diverse linguistic contexts, making reliable detection in languages beyond English essential. The paper's findings have practical implications for building more robust and trustworthy multilingual LLM applications. The success in low-resource languages highlights the potential for transfer learning and resource-efficient approaches. However, the focus on just two LLMs, while understandable for a competition setting, limits the generalizability of the findings.

*   **Strengths:**

    *   **Detailed Ablation Study:** The paper includes a comprehensive set of experiments exploring different prompting strategies (zero-shot, few-shot), translation methods (no translation, external translator, LLM translator), and the use of hypotheses generated by other LLMs.
    *   **Emphasis on Low-Resource Languages:** The paper specifically addresses the challenges of hallucination detection in low-resource languages and demonstrates the effectiveness of their approach in these settings.
    *   **Clear Presentation of Results:** The results are presented in well-organized tables, making it easy to compare the performance of different configurations.
    *   **Reproducibility:** While full reproducibility requires access to the LLMs and Google Translate API, the detailed description of the prompts and methods should enable other researchers to replicate the key findings.

*   **Weaknesses:**

    *   **Limited LLM Selection:** The evaluation is limited to Llama 3 and Claude. While these are strong models, exploring a broader range of LLMs would increase the generalizability of the findings.
    *   **Reliance on External API:** The reliance on Google Translate API introduces a dependency and potentially adds latency to the system. The paper could have explored other translation options, including open-source models.
    *   **Limited Error Analysis:** While the paper categorizes hallucinations, a more in-depth error analysis would provide valuable insights into the types of errors the system still makes and how to address them.
    *   **Lack of Comparison to SOTA:** There is limited comparison with state-of-the-art approaches, which hinders the assessment of the method's relative performance compared to the broader literature. The baselines are relatively simple.

*   **Potential Influence:** The paper's findings will likely influence future research on multilingual hallucination detection, particularly in resource-constrained settings. The detailed comparison of prompting and translation techniques provides valuable guidance for other researchers working in this area.

*   **Rigorous Rationale:** The paper showcases a system tackling a real-world problem (hallucination detection) across multiple languages, which is highly relevant. The findings demonstrate that LLM prompting combined with translation can perform well across languages. The weaknesses discussed limit how the paper could be influential.

**Score: 7**

The paper presents a useful system and makes a practical contribution to multilingual hallucination detection, particularly in low-resource languages. The detailed ablation study provides valuable insights into effective prompting and translation strategies. However, the limited LLM selection, reliance on an external API, absence of comparison to the SOTA methods, and lack of in-depth error analysis prevent it from being a truly groundbreaking contribution.

- **Classification**: cs.CL
- **Score**: 7/10

### AILS-NTUA at SemEval-2025 Task 4: Parameter-Efficient Unlearning for Large Language Models using Data Chunking
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2503.02443v1)
- **Authors**: Iraklis Premptis, Maria Lymperaiou, Giorgos Filandrianos, Orfeas Menis Mastromichalakis, Athanasios Voulodimos, Giorgos Stamou
- **Abstract**: The Unlearning Sensitive Content from Large Language Models task aims to remove targeted datapoints from trained models while minimally affecting their general knowledge. In our work, we leverage parameter-efficient, gradient-based unlearning using low-rank (LoRA) adaptation and layer-focused fine-tuning. To further enhance unlearning effectiveness, we employ data chunking, splitting forget data into disjoint partitions and merging them with cyclically sampled retain samples at a pre-defined ratio. Our task-agnostic method achieves an outstanding forget-retain balance, ranking first on leaderboards and significantly outperforming baselines and competing systems.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the paper:

**Summary:**

The paper presents a parameter-efficient unlearning method for large language models (LLMs) based on data chunking and gradient difference. The key idea is to split the "forget" data into small chunks and process them sequentially, interleaved with retain data, using a combination of gradient ascent (on the forget data) and gradient descent (on the retain data) to minimize catastrophic forgetting. The approach utilizes Low-Rank Adaptation (LoRA) or layer-focused fine-tuning to further reduce computational costs.  The authors ranked first in the SemEval-2025 Task 4, demonstrating superior performance in balancing forgetfulness and knowledge retention compared to baseline methods. The paper explores several hyperparameter configurations including different chunk sizes, forget-to-retain ratios, and LoRA configurations.  They also experiment with fully stochastic gradient updates (batch size of 1) that provide interesting insights.

**Critical Evaluation:**

*   **Novelty:** The core idea of using data chunking within a gradient-based unlearning approach is moderately novel. While gradient ascent for unlearning has been explored before, the specific combination with data chunking and careful management of retain/forget ratios to mitigate catastrophic forgetting provides a unique angle. The exploration of fully stochastic gradient updates is also a distinctive element.

*   **Significance:** The paper's significance lies in its practical effectiveness.  The high performance achieved in the SemEval task demonstrates that this method is a competitive approach to unlearning in LLMs. The focus on parameter efficiency is crucial for applying these techniques to large models. The findings regarding the importance of the forget-to-retain ratio, and chunk size contribute valuable insights for practitioners.
*   **Strengths:**
    *   **Empirical Results:** The paper presents strong empirical evidence of the method's effectiveness, based on the SemEval task.
    *   **Parameter Efficiency:** The use of LoRA and layer-focused fine-tuning makes the approach scalable to larger models.
    *   **Detailed Ablation Studies:** The paper includes a range of ablation studies that help understanding of the impact of various hyperparameters like chunk size. The exploration of fully stochastic gradient updates is interesting, although not fully exploited.
    *   **Task Agnostic Design**: The simplicity of the method, which operates without task-specific fine-tuning suggests a degree of generalizability.

*   **Weaknesses:**
    *   **Limited Exploration of Alternatives**: The paper primarily focuses on gradient difference and briefly experiments with an alternating gradient ascent/descent approach. A deeper exploration and comparison to other advanced unlearning techniques (e.g., negative preference optimization, influence functions) would have strengthened the paper.
    *   **Fluency Issues**: The qualitative results highlight a significant limitation: a loss of fluency in generated text and convergence to repetitive outputs.  This is a notable drawback that needs further investigation, as the model produces nonsensical output. While the ablation of fully stochastic gradients shows the importance of it to the models output.
    *   **Limited Theoretical Analysis**: The paper lacks a theoretical analysis to explain why chunking and gradient difference are effective for unlearning. A deeper theoretical justification would strengthen the paper's contributions.
    *   **Reproducibility Concerns:** The provided code is not fully specified in the original version of the review.

*   **Potential Influence:** The paper provides a practical and effective approach to unlearning that could be adopted by other researchers and practitioners.  The identified challenges (fluency issues) can motivate future research directions.

**Justification for Score:**

The paper makes a solid contribution to the field of machine unlearning. Its strength lies in its strong empirical results and parameter-efficient design. It convincingly demonstrated that the data chunking, gradient different approach could reduce catastrophic forgetting. However, the limitations in terms of fluency, theoretical analysis, and exploration of alternative methods prevent it from being a truly groundbreaking contribution. Therefore, a score of 7 is appropriate, reflecting the valuable and practical contribution while acknowledging the areas for further improvement.

**Score: 7**

- **Classification**: cs.CL
- **Score**: 7/10

### BRIDGE: Bootstrapping Text to Control Time-Series Generation via Multi-Agent Iterative Optimization and Diffusion Modelling
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2503.02445v1)
- **Authors**: Hao Li, Yu-Hao Huang, Chang Xu, Viktor Schlegel, Ren-He Jiang, Riza Batista-Navarro, Goran Nenadic, Jiang Bian
- **Abstract**: Time-series Generation (TSG) is a prominent research area with broad applications in simulations, data augmentation, and counterfactual analysis. While existing methods have shown promise in unconditional single-domain TSG, real-world applications demand for cross-domain approaches capable of controlled generation tailored to domain-specific constraints and instance-level requirements. In this paper, we argue that text can provide semantic insights, domain information and instance-specific temporal patterns, to guide and improve TSG. We introduce ``Text-Controlled TSG'', a task focused on generating realistic time series by incorporating textual descriptions. To address data scarcity in this setting, we propose a novel LLM-based Multi-Agent framework that synthesizes diverse, realistic text-to-TS datasets. Furthermore, we introduce BRIDGE, a hybrid text-controlled TSG framework that integrates semantic prototypes with text description for supporting domain-level guidance. This approach achieves state-of-the-art generation fidelity on 11 of 12 datasets, and improves controllability by 12.52% on MSE and 6.34% MAE compared to no text input generation, highlighting its potential for generating tailored time-series data.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the paper "BRIDGE: Bootstrapping Text to Control Time-Series Generation via Multi-Agent Iterative Optimization and Diffusion Modelling."

**Summary:**

The paper addresses the challenging problem of controlled time-series generation (TSG), particularly in cross-domain settings where the generated time series must adhere to both domain-specific constraints and instance-level requirements. The authors argue that incorporating textual descriptions can provide semantic insights to guide and improve TSG. They introduce a "Text-Controlled TSG" task and propose a novel LLM-based Multi-Agent framework to generate high-quality text-TS datasets. Furthermore, they present BRIDGE, a hybrid framework that integrates semantic prototypes with text descriptions to support domain-level guidance within a diffusion model. The results demonstrate state-of-the-art generation fidelity and improved controllability compared to methods without text input.

**Critical Evaluation:**

*   **Novelty:** The paper presents several novel components. The multi-agent framework for generating text-controlled TSG datasets is a significant contribution, addressing the data scarcity problem in this area. The hybrid approach, BRIDGE, combining semantic prototypes and textual descriptions within a diffusion model is also innovative. The introduction of the "Text-Controlled TSG" task itself is a valuable framing of the problem.

*   **Significance:** TSG has broad applications, and the ability to control the generation process using text is highly desirable for real-world applications. The demonstrated improvements in generation fidelity and controllability suggest that the proposed methods could have a substantial impact on various domains, including healthcare, finance, and beyond.
* **Significance of results:** The improvements are significant given the broadness of the datasets assessed.
*   **Strengths:**
    *   The paper tackles a relevant and complex problem.
    *   The proposed solutions are well-motivated and technically sound.
    *   The multi-agent framework for dataset creation is a clever approach to address data scarcity.
    *   The experimental results demonstrate the effectiveness of the proposed methods across multiple datasets.
    *   The paper provides a detailed analysis of the impact of different text types and agent strategies.
*   **Weaknesses:**
    *   The datasets are generated automatically which may be a bias in the results. Further evaluation on existing human generated text datasets is important.
    *   While human evaluation is included, more detailed qualitative analysis of the generated time series, including case studies demonstrating the benefits of text-controlled generation, could strengthen the paper.
    *   The reliance on LLMs introduces potential biases and limitations related to their pre-training data and capabilities. The paper could benefit from a discussion of these limitations and how they might be mitigated.

*   **Potential Impact:** The paper has the potential to advance the field of TSG by enabling more controlled and tailored generation of time series data. The proposed methods could be used to create synthetic data for training machine learning models, simulating various scenarios, and generating counterfactual explanations.

**Justification for Score:**

The paper presents a solid contribution to the field of time-series generation. The novel multi-agent framework and hybrid text-enhanced generation strategy (BRIDGE) address a significant gap in the existing literature. The empirical results support the effectiveness of the proposed methods, demonstrating improved fidelity and controllability. While the paper has some limitations, such as the reliance on pre-trained LLMs and the need for more qualitative analysis, the overall contribution is substantial and warrants a high score.

### Measuring What Makes You Unique: Difference-Aware User Modeling for Enhancing LLM Personalization
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2503.02450v1)
- **Authors**: Yilun Qiu, Xiaoyan Zhao, Yang Zhang, Yimeng Bai, Wenjie Wang, Hong Cheng, Fuli Feng, Tat-Seng Chua
- **Abstract**: Personalizing Large Language Models (LLMs) has become a critical step in facilitating their widespread application to enhance individual life experiences. In pursuit of personalization, distilling key preference information from an individual's historical data as instructional preference context to customize LLM generation has emerged as a promising direction. However, these methods face a fundamental limitation by overlooking the inter-user comparative analysis, which is essential for identifying the inter-user differences that truly shape preferences. To address this limitation, we propose Difference-aware Personalization Learning (DPL), a novel approach that emphasizes extracting inter-user differences to enhance LLM personalization. DPL strategically selects representative users for comparison and establishes a structured standard to extract meaningful, task-relevant differences for customizing LLM generation. Extensive experiments on real-world datasets demonstrate that DPL significantly enhances LLM personalization. We release our code at https://github.com/SnowCharmQ/DPL.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the provided paper:

**Summary:**

The paper "Measuring What Makes You Unique: Difference-Aware User Modeling for Enhancing LLM Personalization" addresses a key limitation in current Large Language Model (LLM) personalization techniques: the lack of inter-user comparative analysis. Existing methods primarily focus on extracting and injecting user-specific information without considering how a user's preferences differ from others. The authors propose a novel approach called Difference-aware Personalization Learning (DPL). DPL strategically selects representative users for comparison, establishes a structured standard for extracting task-relevant differences along predefined dimensions (writing style, emotional tone, semantic content), and then uses these differences to customize LLM generation. The paper validates DPL on a review generation task, demonstrating improved personalization compared to existing baselines through various experimental evaluations and ablation studies.

**Critical Evaluation:**

*   **Novelty:** The core idea of incorporating inter-user *differences* into LLM personalization is a significant and valuable contribution. While previous work has focused on user-specific data, explicitly modeling differences is a novel approach that aligns with psychological principles of uniqueness shaping preferences. The structured difference extraction is also a valuable addition, preventing irrelevant information from clouding the process. However, using clustering to select users for comparison is not particularly novel, though practical.

*   **Significance:** Personalizing LLMs is crucial for their widespread adoption and effectiveness in diverse applications. By enhancing personalization, this work contributes to making LLMs more relevant and helpful for individual users. The performance gains demonstrated in the experiments, particularly on the real-world Amazon Reviews dataset, highlight the practical significance of the approach. Further, the clear evaluation methodology using both conventional and LLM-based metrics strengthens the findings.

*   **Strengths:**

    *   **Strong conceptual foundation:** The paper presents a clear and well-motivated argument for the importance of inter-user differences in personalization.
    *   **Well-defined method:** DPL is clearly explained with appropriate formulations and architectural descriptions.
    *   **Comprehensive experiments:** The experiments are thorough and cover various aspects of the proposed method, including ablation studies, hyperparameter analysis, and comparisons with strong baselines.
    *   **Code Availability:**  Providing the code promotes reproducibility and further research.

*   **Weaknesses:**

    *   **Limited task:** The evaluation is primarily focused on the review generation task. While a representative task, demonstrating DPL's effectiveness across different types of personalization scenarios would strengthen the generalization of the results.
    *   **Predefined Dimensions:** The choice of writing style, emotional tone, and semantic content, although reasonable, may be limited. Adapting the dimensions dynamically based on the task or user profile could lead to further improvements.
    *   **Computational Cost:**  While the paper addresses the efficiency by selecting representative users using clustering, comparing each user to a select few might still involve significant computational costs in high-volume online applications. A closer analysis of the time complexity of the entire DPL process, with an exploration of the different optimization opportunities, is needed.
    *   **Hyperparameter Tuning:** While the impact of the 'K' value is explored, a more systematic, perhaps even automated approach to hyperparameter tuning could potentially yield better results.
    *   **Explainability:** While the paper offers some insight, a more detailed study on the explainability of DPL's personalized outputs, with analyses of how extracted differences influence generated texts, will be interesting.

*   **Potential Influence:** This work has the potential to influence future research on LLM personalization by shifting the focus from simply incorporating user-specific data to considering inter-user differences. The proposed DPL method provides a concrete framework for extracting and leveraging these differences, which can serve as a starting point for further exploration.

**Justification for Score:**

The paper presents a novel and well-executed approach to LLM personalization, addressing a significant limitation in existing methods. The experimental results demonstrate the effectiveness of the proposed method on the review generation task, supported by comprehensive evaluations. The primary weaknesses include the limited scope of the task and potential computational costs. However, the strengths outweigh the weaknesses, making this a valuable contribution with the potential to influence future research in this area.

### Don't Get Too Excited -- Eliciting Emotions in LLMs
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2503.02457v1)
- **Authors**: Gino Franco Fazzi, Julie Skoven Hinge, Stefan Heinrich, Paolo Burelli
- **Abstract**: This paper investigates the challenges of affect control in large language models (LLMs), focusing on their ability to express appropriate emotional states during extended dialogues. We evaluated state-of-the-art open-weight LLMs to assess their affective expressive range in terms of arousal and valence. Our study employs a novel methodology combining LLM-based sentiment analysis with multiturn dialogue simulations between LLMs. We quantify the models' capacity to express a wide spectrum of emotions and how they fluctuate during interactions. Our findings reveal significant variations among LLMs in their ability to maintain consistent affect, with some models demonstrating more stable emotional trajectories than others. Furthermore, we identify key challenges in affect control, including difficulties in producing and maintaining extreme emotional states and limitations in adapting affect to changing conversational contexts. These findings have important implications for the development of more emotionally intelligent AI systems and highlight the need for improved affect modelling in LLMs.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the paper:

**Summary:**

The paper "Don't Get Too Excited - Eliciting Emotions in LLMs" explores the challenges of affect control in large language models (LLMs). It investigates the ability of open-weight LLMs to express appropriate emotional states, specifically focusing on Valence and Arousal, during multi-turn dialogues. The study combines LLM-based sentiment analysis with dialogue simulations between LLMs. The authors quantify the models' ability to convey a range of emotions and track their fluctuations during interactions.  The findings highlight variations in LLM's abilities to maintain consistent affect, difficulties in generating extreme emotions, and limitations adapting to context shifts.

**Critical Evaluation:**

**Novelty:** The paper introduces a novel methodology combining LLM-based sentiment analysis with multi-turn LLM dialogue simulations. This approach allows for a quantitative analysis of how LLMs elicit Valence and Arousal during conversations, which goes beyond simply classifying emotion in static text. The study also provides a qualitative analysis of how conditioned and unconditioned LLMs maintain emotional states during exchanges. This provides insights into the interaction between LLMs regarding their respective emotional states.

**Significance:** The paper's significance lies in addressing the crucial aspect of emotional intelligence in AI systems, particularly in conversational agents. By investigating how LLMs can express and maintain emotional states, the research contributes to the development of more natural and empathetic AI interactions. The identification of key challenges in affect control, such as difficulties generating extreme emotions and adapting to context, is a valuable contribution to the field. Highlighting the problem is a necessary initial step to spur further work toward emotion control of LLMs.

**Strengths:**

*   **Novel Methodology:** The combination of LLM-based sentiment analysis and multi-turn dialogue simulation is a strong methodological contribution.
*   **Quantitative Analysis:**  Quantifying Valence and Arousal provides a measurable way to assess the emotional expression of LLMs.
*   **Qualitative Insights:** The analysis of LLM behavior and convergence in emotional states offers valuable insights into their interaction dynamics.
*   **Focus on Open-Weight Models:** The exclusive use of open-weight LLMs enhances the reproducibility and accessibility of the research.

**Weaknesses:**

*   **Reliance on Automated Sentiment Analysis:** The reliance on automated sentiment analysis, even with validated models, introduces potential inaccuracies and biases compared to human evaluation. While the automated methods are correlated to human evaluation, this correlation is not one-to-one and reduces the certainty of the accuracy of the model results.
*   **Limited Context Complexity:** The experimental setup uses relatively simple dialogue scenarios. The ability of LLMs to manage affect in more complex, real-world conversations remains an open question.
*   **Lack of Human-in-the-Loop Validation:** The absence of human evaluation limits the ability to determine if the emotional expressions generated by the LLMs are perceived as genuine or appropriate by human users.
*   **Potential for Bias:** Although the study employs different combinations of name, age and nationality, there is still a potential for bias regarding nationality, or gender.

**Potential Influence:**

The paper has the potential to influence the field of affective computing and conversational AI by:

*   Providing a framework for evaluating affect control in LLMs.
*   Highlighting the importance of emotional consistency and adaptation in conversational agents.
*   Inspiring further research into more sophisticated affect modeling techniques for LLMs.
*   Spurring development of new methods to improve the emotional expressiveness of LLMs in interactions.

**Overall:**

The paper addresses an important problem with a novel and measurable approach to quantifying affect in LLMs. While the study would benefit from human validation and more complex conversational contexts, it presents valuable insights into the challenges of achieving emotional intelligence in AI. The study would also benefit from bias evaluation, which is mentioned to exist within current LLM practices.

### Exploring Token-Level Augmentation in Vision Transformer for Semi-Supervised Semantic Segmentation
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2503.02459v1)
- **Authors**: Dengke Zhang, Quan Tang, Fagui Liu, C. L. Philip Chen, Haiqing Mei
- **Abstract**: Semi-supervised semantic segmentation has witnessed remarkable advancements in recent years. However, existing algorithms are based on convolutional neural networks and directly applying them to Vision Transformers poses certain limitations due to conceptual disparities. To this end, we propose TokenMix, a data augmentation technique specifically designed for semi-supervised semantic segmentation with Vision Transformers. TokenMix aligns well with the global attention mechanism by mixing images at the token level, enhancing learning capability for contexutual information among image patches. We further incorporate image augmentation and feature augmentation to promote the diversity of augmentation. Moreover, to enhance consistency regularization, we propose a dual-branch framework where each branch applies both image augmentation and feature augmentation to the input image. We conduct extensive experiments across multiple benchmark datasets, including Pascal VOC 2012, Cityscapes, and COCO. Results suggest that the proposed method outperforms state-of-the-art algorithms with notably observed accuracy improvement, especially under the circumstance of limited fine annotations.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the paper:

**Summary:**

The paper introduces "TokenMix," a novel data augmentation technique specifically designed for semi-supervised semantic segmentation using Vision Transformers (ViTs).  It addresses limitations of applying CNN-based S4 methods directly to ViTs by operating at the token level, thereby aligning better with ViT's patch-based processing. TokenMix exchanges tokens between images to introduce new contextual information, improving the understanding of patch relationships. The authors also incorporate image and feature augmentation in a dual-branch framework to further enhance augmentation diversity and consistency regularization. Experimental results on PASCAL VOC 2012, Cityscapes, and COCO datasets demonstrate improved performance compared to existing state-of-the-art methods, particularly when labeled data is limited.

**Critical Evaluation:**

*   **Novelty:** The core idea of TokenMix – token-level mixing for ViTs in semi-supervised segmentation – appears to be novel. It directly addresses a recognized gap in adapting CNN-centric S4 methods to the architectural differences of ViTs. The dual-branch framework employing both image and feature augmentation for consistency regularization is a relatively standard technique in S4, but its combination with TokenMix and its application within the ViT context contributes to the overall novelty. This method is a notable improvement to existing methods, like CutMix, to create a more appropriate method for token-based architectures.
*   **Significance:** The significance lies in bridging the gap between established S4 techniques and the growing popularity of ViTs. S4 is crucial for reducing annotation costs, and ViTs offer powerful global modeling capabilities. An effective way to leverage ViTs in the S4 framework would likely improve segmentation performance, particularly with limited labeled data. The experiments validate this potential, showing competitive results, especially in low-data regimes. This helps to reduce labor costs, a notable issue in semantic segmentation.
*   **Strengths:**
    *   **Problem-focused approach:** The paper directly tackles the problem of adapting S4 to ViTs, addressing specific conceptual differences.
    *   **Technical soundess:** The TokenMix technique is well-motivated and aligns logically with ViT's architecture. The dual-branch framework enhances the method.
    *   **Strong experimental validation:** Experiments across multiple datasets (Pascal, Cityscapes, COCO) provide compelling evidence of the method's effectiveness. The ablation studies give insights into the impact of different design choices.
    *   **Clarity:** The paper is well-written and presents the method and experimental setup clearly.
*   **Weaknesses:**
    *   **Incremental improvement:** While effective, the performance gains may be considered incremental rather than revolutionary. The gains on some datasets are relatively modest compared to other, potentially simpler, approaches.
    *   **Limited theoretical analysis:** While the method is intuitive, a deeper theoretical analysis could strengthen the contribution, explaining why TokenMix is more effective for ViTs.
    *   **Computational cost:** The paper does not explicitly discuss the computational overhead introduced by TokenMix and the dual-branch framework. A comparison of training time and resource requirements would be helpful.

**Justification for the Score:**

The paper makes a solid contribution to the field. TokenMix is a novel and technically sound adaptation of data augmentation for ViT-based semi-supervised segmentation. It addresses a specific problem in a logical and effective way, as demonstrated by the experiments. While the gains might not be groundbreaking, the approach is valuable for researchers working with ViTs in semi-supervised settings. It’s a well-executed and clearly presented piece of work.
